   1. The abbreviation GSM stands for
      GSM flá fhÿu Ndú; jkafka

          1)    Global System for Mobile Access.
          2)    Global System for Mobile Communication.
          3)    Global System for Mobile Phone.
          4)    Global System for Mobile Transmission.
          5)    Global System for Mobile Interconnection.
                                                                                                                                                         ( 2011 – 06 )
          Answer: 2)

          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

          Option/ úl,amh 1)
          While this option sounds somewhat correct, it is not the official meaning of the GSM abbreviation.
          “Mobile Access” is a general term and does not reflect the actual standard defined by telecommunications
          authorities. GSM is a specific technology standard, and this term is inaccurate in that context.
          fuu úl,amh ;rula ksjerÈ f,i fmkqko" th GSM hk flá fhÿfï ks, w¾:h fkdfõ' Mobile
          Access hkq idudkH fhÿula jk w;r úÿ,s ixfoaY n,OdÍka úiska w¾: olajd we;s ienE m%ñ;sh
          ms<sìUq fkdlrhs' GSM hkq ksYaÑ; ;dlaIKsl m%ñ;shla jk w;r" tu ikao¾Nh ;=< fuu fhÿu
          idjoH fõ'

          Option/ úl,amh 2)
          GSM stands for Global System for Mobile Communication. It is the standard developed to describe the
          protocols for second-generation (2G) digital cellular networks used by mobile phones. It is widely adopted
          around the world and supports voice calls, SMS, and limited data services. This is the accurate and
          complete definition, making it the correct answer.
          GSM hkq Global System for Mobile Communication fõ' th cx.u ÿrl:k Ndú;d lrk fojk
          mrïmrdfõ ^2G& äðg,a fi,shq,¾ cd, i|yd jk kshudj,s úia;r lsÍu i|yd ixj¾Okh lrk ,o
          m%ñ;shhs' th f,dj mqrd nyq,j Ndú;d jk w;r weu;=ï" flá mKsjqv iy iSñ; o;a; fiajd i|yd
          iyh olajhs'

          Option/ úl,amh 3)
          GSM is not just for mobile phones; it also defines the entire system for communication between devices,
          including infrastructure like base stations and switching systems. “Mobile Phone” is too narrow a
          description for the scope of GSM. So, this is not the right answer.
          GSM hkq cx.u ÿrl:k i|yd muKla fkdfõ th uQ,sl ia:dk iy udre lsÍfï moaO;s jeks há;,
          myiqlï we;=¿j Wmdx. w;r ikaksfõokh i|yd iuia; moaO;shu ks¾jpkh lrhs' cx.u ÿrl:kh˜
          hkq GSM ys úIh m:h i|yd b;d mgq úia;rhls'

          Option/ úl,amh 4)
          While GSM does involve transmission of signals, the term “Mobile Transmission” is too vague and
          imprecise. The correct term should emphasize communication, which includes both sending and receiving
          voice and data.
          GSM ix{d iïfm%aIKh we;=<;a jqjo" cx.u iïfm%aIKh˜ hk fhÿu b;d wmeyeÈ,s iy wmeyeÈ,s
          h' ksjerÈ moh ikaksfõokh wjOdrKh l< hq;= w;r" thg o;a; heùu iy ,eîu hk folu
          we;=<;a fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -2-                                          #ictfromabc



    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    Interconnection refers to the linkage of different networks or systems, but GSM defines much more than
    that. It includes protocols, security, frequency bands, and communication rules. “Interconnection” does
    not reflect the broader purpose of GSM, which is communication.
    wka;¾ iïnkaO;djh hkq úúO cd, fyda moaO;s iïnkaO lsÍu hkakhs" kuq;a GSM Bg jvd fndfyda
    foa w¾: olajhs' thg kshudj,s" wdrlaIdj" ixLHd; l,dm iy ikaksfõok kS;s we;=<;a fõ' wka;¾
    iïnkaO;djh hkq ikaksfõokh jk GSM ys mq¿,a wruqK ms<sìUq fkdlrhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)

    muKla fkdj ud¾.h fidhd .ekSuhs'

 4. What is the main function of a DHCP server?
    DHCP fiajdodhl mß.Klhl (Server) m%Odk ld¾hh l=ula o?

    1) Allocating IP addresses
       IP ,smsk ,nd §u
    2) Resolving domain names from IP addresses
       IP ,smskj,ska jiï kdu (Domain name) ,nd §u
    3) Providing directory services to users

    4) Sharing an Internet connection among users
       mßYs,lhska w;r wka;¾cd, iïnkaO;djla yjq,g nqla;s ú£ug ie,eiaùu
    5) Protecting a computer network from virus attacks.
       jhsria m%ydrj,ska (Virus attacks) mß.Kl cd,hla wdrlaId lsÍ

                                                                                              ( 2011 – 16 )
    Answer: 1)
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Option/ úl,amh 1)

    fuh DHCP ^.;sl i;aldrl úkHdi fm%dfgdaflda,h& ys m%Odk ld¾hNdrhhs'
    It automatically assigns IP addresses to devices on a network when they join.

    thg fmrksñ oajdrh" Wmcd, wdjrKh iy DNS fiajdodhlh jeks wfkl=;a cd, ieliqïo iemhsh
    yelsh'

    DNS translates human-readable domain names (like www.example.com) into machine-readable IP
    addresses. / DNS udkj-lshúh yels jiï kdu ^www.example.com jeks& hka;%-lshúh yels IP ,smsk
    njg mßj¾;kh lrhs'

    iy Wmdx. ixúOdkh lsÍug iy l<ukdlrKh lsÍug WmldÍ fõ'

    Not related to DHCP
    DHCP yd iïnkaO fkdfõ'

 5. Consider the IP address 192.248.87.3 and the subnet mask 255.255.255.224. How many hosts can
    be directly connected to this network
    192.248.87.3 hk IP ,smskh yd 255.255.255.224 wkqcd, jikh (Subnet mask) i,lkak' fuu
    cd,hg i;aldrlhska lS fokl= iDcqju iïnkaO l< yels o?

    1) 16               2) 24               3) 30               4) 64                5) 128

                                                                                                    ( 2011 – 17 )
    Answer: 3)
    IP Address: 192.248.87.3
    Subnet Mask: 255.255.255.224

    Step 1:
    Understand the subnet mask
    Wmcd, wdjrKh f;areï .kak

    255.255.255.224 in binary
    oaúuh jYfhka 255.255.255.224

    255 = 11111111
    255 = 11111111
    255 = 11111111
    224 = 11100000
    So the full subnet mask in binary is
    tneúka oaúuh ;=< iïmQ¾K Wmcd, wdjrKh
    11111111.11111111.11111111.11100000
    This means
    fuhska woyia jkafka
    Number of bits for the network: 27 (eight 1s in each of the first three octets + 3 bits from the last one)
    cd,h i|yd ìgq .Kk( 27 ^m<uq wIagl ;=fkka tla tla 1s wgla + wjidk tflka ìgq 3&

    Number of bits for the host : 32 - 27 = 5
    Odrlh i|yd ìgq .Kk : 32 - 27 = 5

    Step 2:
    Calculate number of usable hosts
    Ndú;d l< yels Odrl .Kk .Kkh lsÍu

    Use the formula
    iQ;%h Ndú;d lruq
    Number of hosts = 2^number of host bits - 2
                    = 2^5 - 2
                   = 32 - 2
                   = 30

    We subtract 2 because
    wms 2 wvq lrkafka ukao

    1 IP is reserved for network address
    1 IP ,smskh cd, ,smskh i|yd fjka lr we;

    1 IP is reserved for broadcast address
    1 IP úldYk ,smskh i|yd fjka lr we;
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -7-                                       #ictfromabc



    Correct Answer: (3) 30

    This means 30 devices can be connected directly to the subnet 192.248.87.3 /27.
    fuhska woyia jkafka Wmdx. 30 la Wm cd,hg iDcqju iïnkaO l< yels njhs 192.248.87.3 /27.

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 6. What is the most suitable media to transmit a high definition video over a distance of 5 km?
    5km ÿrla wdjrKh jk mßÈ wê úfNaok ùäfhdajla (High definition video) iïfma%IKh lsÍu i|yd
    jvd;a iqÿiq udOHh l=ula o?

    4)   Coaxial cables / taldlaI flan,

                                                                                             ( 2011 – 18 )
    Answer: 3)
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Option/ úl,amh 1)

    Wireless medium.
    /yeka rys; udOHh'

    Good for broadcasting over long distances without cables.
    flan,a fkdue;sj È.= ÿrla úldYkh lsÍu i|yd fyd|hs'

    unless you use specialized high-frequency systems (e.g., microwave, satellite).
    flfia fj;;a" Tn úfYaIs; wê-ixLHd; moaO;s ^Wod( uhsfl%dafõõ" pkaøsld& Ndú;d fkdlrkafka kï"
    ix{d msßySu iy ndOd lsÍï fya;=fjka lsf,daógr 5 g jeä by< l,dm m<, HD ùäfhda i|yd iqÿiq
    fkdfõ'

    Common in LANs (e.g., Ethernet).

    Maximum reliable distance is 100 meters for high-speed data (Cat 5e/6).
    wêfõ.S o;a; i|yd Wmßu úYajdiodhl ÿr óg¾ 100 ls (Cat 5e/6).

    Not suitable for 5 km or high-definition video due to signal attenuation and limited bandwidth.

    iqÿiq fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    Uses light to transmit data — very high speed and large bandwidth.
    o;a; iïfm%aIKh lsÍug wdf,dalh Ndú;d lrhs - b;d by< fõ.hla iy úYd, l,dm m<,la'

    Supports long distances — tens of kilometers — without significant signal loss.
    ie,lsh hq;= ix{d w,dNhlska f;drj - lsf,daóg¾ oi oyia .Kkla - È.= ÿr i|yd iyh olajhs'

    Ideal for HD video transmission over 5 km.
    lsf,daógr 5 g jeä HD ùäfhda iïfm%aIKh i|yd jvd;a iqÿiqh'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -8-                                          #ictfromabc



    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Used in cable TV and CCTV.
    flan,a àù iy iSiSàù j, Ndú;d fõ'

 7. The transport layer of the OSI reference model provides
    OSI ks¾foaY wdlD;sfhys (Reference model) m%jdyk ;,h (Transport layer) u.ska imhkq ,nkafka

    1)   error correction / fodaI ksjerÈ lsßuhs'
    2)   routing of data packets / o;a; u¨ ud¾..; lsÍuhs'
    3)   flow control / .e,Sï md,khhs'
    4)   process-to-process communication / l%shdj,s-isg-l%shdj,s ikaksfõokhhs'
    5)   error detection / fodaI wkdjrKhhs'
                                                                                                 ( 2011 – 19 )
    Answer: 3)

    Functions of the Transport Layer / m%jdyk ia:rfha ld¾hhka
    The Transport Layer (Layer 4 of the OSI model) is responsible for reliable data transfer between two
    processes running on different hosts.
    m%jdyk ia:rh ^OSI wdlD;sfha 4 jk ia:rh& úúO Odrl u; l%shd;aul jk l%shdj,Ska folla w;r
    úYajdiodhl o;a; yqjudrej i|yd j.lsj hq;=h'

    Flow Control – manages the pace at which data is transmitted, ensuring that the sender does not
    overwhelm the receiver.
    m%jdy md,kh - o;a; iïfm%aIKh jk fõ.h l<ukdlrKh lrhs" hjkakd .%dylhd hgm;a
    fkdlrk nj iy;sl lrhs'

    Error Control – detects and corrects errors in the transmitted data (but not to be confused with error

    ia:rh jeks wfkl=;a ia:rj, isÿ lrk fodaI y÷kd.ekSu iuÕ mg,jd fkd.; hq;=h&'

    Segmentation and Reassembly – splits large data streams into smaller segments and reassembles them at

    wjidkfha tAjd kej; tl,ia lrhs'

   1. In computer networks, acronym DHCP stands for
      mß.Kl cd,j, DHCP yel=¿fuka woyia jkafka

          1) Dynamic Host Control Protocol.                                                       2) Dynamic Host Configuration Protocol.
          3) Domain Host Configuration Protocol.                                                  4) Dynamic Host Configuration Practice.
          5) Dynamic Host Control Parameters.
                                                                                                                                                         ( 2012 – 06 )
          Answer: 2)

          Correct Answer / ksjerÈ ms<s;=r
          2) Dynamic Host Configuration Protocol
          Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
          DHCP is a network protocol used to automatically assign IP addresses and other network configuration
          settings (like subnet mask, gateway, and DNS server) to devices on a network.
          DHCP hkq cd,hl Wmdx.j,g IP ,smsk iy wfkl=;a cd, úkHdi ieliqï ^Wmcd, wdjrKh" oajdrh
          iy ^DNS fiajdodhlh jeks& iajhxl%Shj mejÍug Ndú;d lrk cd, fm%dfgdaflda,hls'
          This helps devices join a network without manual configuration.
          w;ska úkHdi lsÍulska f;drj Wmdx. cd,hlg iïnkaO ùug fuh WmldÍ fõ'
          Dynamic: IP addresses are assigned automatically and can change.
          .;sl( IP ,smsk iajhxl%Shj mjrd we;s w;r fjkia úh yelsh'
          Host: Refers to any device (computer, phone, etc.) connecting to the network.
          i;aldrl( cd,hg iïnkaO jk ´kEu Wmdx.hla ^mß.Klh" ÿrl:kh" wdÈh& fj; fhduq fõ'
          Configuration Protocol: It provides the settings (like IP, gateway) needed for communication.
          úkHdi fm%dfgdaflda,h( th ikaksfõokh i|yd wjYH ieliqï ^IP, oajdrh jeks& imhhs'

     Why the other options are wrong / wfkl=;a úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs
   ➢ Option/ úl,amh 1)
     Wrong word; it’s Configuration, not Control.
     jerÈ jpkh th md,kh fkdj úkHdihhs'

   ➢ Option/ úl,amh 3)
     Incorrect term; DHCP isn't tied to domain names specifically.
     jerÈ moh DHCP úfYaIfhka jiï kdu iuÕ ne£ fkdue;'

   ➢ Option/ úl,amh 4)
     Not a real protocol.
     ienE fm%dfgdaflda,hla fkdfõ'

   ➢ Option/ úl,amh 5)
     Incorrect expansion.
     jerÈ m%idrKh'

         Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
         tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 2. What is the function of a DNS in a Computer Network?

     1)   Assigns IP addresses / IP ,smsk mjrd fohs'

     3)   Protects a network from viruses / cd,hla jhsrij,ska wdrlaId lrhs'

     5)   Connects multiple computer networks together / mß.Kl cd, lsysmhla tlsfklg iïnkaO lrhs'

   IP addresses to devices in a network.

   cd,hl Wmdx.j,g IP ,smsk mjrhs'

   ,smskhlg mßj¾;kh lrhs" th mß.Klh Google ys fiajdodhlhka fidhd .ekSug Ndú;d lrhs'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 4)

   kdudj,s fiajd ^uhsfl%dfid*aÜ ys l%shdldÍ kdudj,sh jeks& mßYS,l .sKqï iy wjir jeks cd, iïm;a
   l<ukdlrKh lsÍug WmldÍ fõ' DNS hkq iïmQ¾K kdudj,s fiajdjla fkdfõ - thg tllg iyh
   oelaúh yelsh'

   or logically connect networks.
   fuh DNS fkdj rjqgrhl fyda oajdrhl N+ñldjhs' DNS kï IP j,g muKla úi|hs - th fN!;slj
   fyda ;d¾lslj cd, iïnkaO fkdlrhs'

     Incorrect — DNS doesn’t connect networks.
     jerÈhs - DNS cd, iïnkaO fkdlrhs'

 16. Which of the following statements is correct about IP addresses and subnet masks?
     IP ,smsk iN wkqcd, wdjrK (subnet masks) ms<sn|j my; olafjk ljr m%ldYh ksjerÈ fõ o?

    1) 192.248.32.3 is a Class B IP address
        192.248.32.3 hkq B mka;sfha IP ,smskhls'
    2) A network with a subnet mask 255.255.255.248 can accommodate six hosts.
        255.255.255.248 wkqcd, wdjrKhla iys; cd,hlg i;aldrlhska 6lg myiqlï ie,ish yels h'
    3) An IP address consists of 16 bits
        IP ,smskhla oajHxl (bits) 16 lska iukaú; fõ'
    4) 10.32.1.5 is a Class C IP address
        10.32.1.5 hkq C mka;sfha IP ,smskhls'
    5) 255.255.255.0 is a Class C IP address
        255.255.255.0 hkq C mka;sfha IP ,smskhls'
                                                                                              ( 2012 – 16 )
    Answer: 2)
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 1)
   Inorrect / jerÈ
   IP Class Ranges :
   Class A: 1.0.0.0 to 126.255.255.255
   Class B: 128.0.0.0 to 191.255.255.255
   Class C: 192.0.0.0 to 223.255.255.255
    192.248.32.3 falls in the Class C range.
    192.248.32.3 C mka;sfha mrdihg wh;a fõ'
    Class C, Not Class B
    B mka;sh fkdj C mka;sh
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -16-                                   #ictfromabc


 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 2)
   Correct / ksjerÈ
   255.255.255.248 = /29 subnet
    Total addresses / uq¿ ,smsk: 2^(32-29) = 2^3 = 8
    Subtract / wvq lsÍu
    1 for network address / cd, ,smskh i|yd 1
    1 for broadcast address / úldYk ,smskh i|yd 1

    Usable hosts / Ndú;d l< yels Odrl = 8 - 2 = 6

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 3)
   Inorrect / jerÈ
   IPv4 addresses consist of 32 bits (divided into 4 octets of 8 bits each)
   IPv4 ,smsk ìgq 32 lska iukaú; fõ ^tla tla ìgq 8 ne.ska jQ wIaGu 4 lg fnod we;&
    Example: 192.168.1.1 → 4 x 8 bits = 32 bits

    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Inorrect / jerÈ
    10.0.0.0 – 10.255.255.255 falls under Class A
    10.0.0.0 – 10.255.255.255 A mka;shg wh;a fõ
    10.32.1.5 is clearly in Class A
    10.32.1.5 meyeÈ,sju A mka;sfha we;

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 5)
   Inorrect / jerÈ
   This is actually a subnet mask, not an IP address.
   fuh we;a; jYfhkau IP ,smskhla fkdj Wmcd, wdjrKhls'
    255.255.255.0 is the default subnet mask for a Class C network.
    255.255.255.0 hkq C mka;sfha C cd,hla i|yd fmrksñ Wmcd, wdjrKhls'
    But the statement says it's an IP address, which is wrong.
    kuq;a m%ldYfha mjikafka th IP ,smskhla njhs" th jerÈhs'

    Therefore, option 2) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 2) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 4. The network layer of the OSI reference model provides
    OSI fhduq wdo¾Yfha cd, ia;rh (network layer)
    1)   error correction / jerÈ ksjerÈlsÍï isÿ lrhs'
    2)   inter-process communication / wka;¾-l%shdj,s ikaksfõokh isÿ lrhs'
    3)   flow control / .e,Sï md,kh isÿ lrhs'
    4)   routing of data packets / o;a; meleÜgq ud¾..; lsÍu (routing) isÿ lrhs'
    5)   error detection / fodaI wkdjrKh isÿ lrhs'
                                                                                          ( 2012 – 17 )
    Answer: 4)

   Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 1)
   Incorrect / jerÈ
   Error correction is handled at the Data Link Layer and sometimes at the Transport Layer (like TCP).
   fodaI ksjerÈ lsÍu o;a; ine¢ ia:rfha§ iy iuyr úg m%jdyk ia:rfha§^TCP jeks& yiqrejkq ,efí'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -17-                                       #ictfromabc


    The Network Layer does not correct errors; it focuses on routing and addressing.
    cd, ia:rh fodaI ksjerÈ fkdlrhs th ud¾..; lsÍu iy wduka;%Kh flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 2)
   Incorrect / jerÈ
   Inter-process communication happens at the Transport Layer (Layer 4), which ensures communication
   between applications or processes.

          What is a Proxy Server?
          fm%dlais fiajdodhlhla hkq l=ulao@

          A proxy server acts as an intermediary between client devices (like your computer) and the wider internet.
          Its main functions include
          fm%dlais fiajdodhlhla fiajdodhl Wmdx. ^Tfí mß.Klh jeks& iy mq¿,a wka;¾cd,h w;r
          w;rueÈfhl= f,i l%shd lrhs' tys m%Odk ld¾hhka w;rg

          •     Filtering content, providing security or monitoring.
                wka;¾.;h fmÍu" wdrlaIdj fyda wëlaIKh iemhSu'
          •     Sharing one internet connection with multiple computers on a local network.
                foaYSh cd,hl nyq mß.Kl iuÕ tla wka;¾cd, iïnkaO;djhla fnod .ekSu'
          So, if multiple devices in a LAN need to access the internet, a proxy server can handle all their requests
          using one public IP address, effectively sharing the internet connection.

 2. An organization has been allocated a Class C IP address range having a subnet mask of
    255.255.255.0. If the organization is to setup a web server and an email server, what are the IP
    addresses that can be allocated to these two servers?
    wdh;khlg 255.255.255.0 f,i we;s Wm cd, wdjrKhla (subnet mask) iys; C mka;sfha IP ,smsk
    mrdihla fjkalr § ;sfí' fuu wdh;khg fjí fiajdodhlhla yd úoHq;a ;eme,a fiajdodhlhla ia:dms;
    lr .; hq;=j we;akï fuu fiajdodhl fol i|yd fjka l< yels ,smsk fudkjd o?

    1)   192.248.87.2, 192.248.32.3

    (2) 192.248.87.4, 192.248.87.5

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    255.255.255.0 Wmcd, wdjrKhla iys; C mka;sfha ,smskhla woyia lrkafka l=ulao@

    A Class C IP address typically starts with 192.x.x.x.
    C mka;sfha C IP ,smskhla idudkHfhka 192.x.x.x. iuÕ wdrïN fõ'

    The subnet mask 255.255.255.0 means
    255.255.255.0 Wmcd, wdjrKfhka woyia jkafka
    •    The first 3 octets (24 bits) define the network.
         m<uq wIaGuh 3 ^ìgq 24& cd,h w¾: olajhs'
    •    The last octet (8 bits) is used for hosts within that network.
         wjidk wIaGuh ^ìgq 8& tu cd,h ;=< we;s Odrlhka i|yd Ndú;d fõ'

    This means all IP addresses in the 192.248.87.0/24 range belong to the same network
    fuhska woyia lrkafka 192.248.87.0/24 mrdifha we;s ish¨u IP ,smsk tlu cd,hlg wh;a njhs

    •   Valid host IP range: 192.248.87.1 to 192.248.87.254
        j,x.= i;aldrl IP mrdih( 192.248.87.1 isg 192.248.87.254 olajd
    •   .0 is the network address; .255 is the broadcast address.
        .0 hkq cd, ,smskhhs .255 hkq úldYk ,smskhhs'

    What kind of IP addresses should be assigned to servers?
    fiajdodhlhkag mejßh hq;af;a l=uk wdldrfha IP ,smsko@

    IPs assigned to the web server and email server must be from the same subnet (same network portion).
    fjí fiajdodhlhg iy úoHq;a ;eme,a fiajdodhlhg mjrd we;s IP ,smsk tlu Wmcd,hlska ^tlu cd,
    fldgilska& úh hq;=h'

    Both IPs should be in the range: 192.248.87.1 to 192.248.87.254
    IP folu 192.248.87.1 isg 192.248.87.254 olajd mrdifha ;sìh hq;=h

   Let’s review the options / úl,am iudf,dapkh lruq

   192.248.32.3 is in a different subnet (not 192.248.87.x)
   192.248.32.3 fjkia Wm cd,hl ^192.248.87.x fkdfõ&

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 2)
   Both addresses are in the same network: 192.248.87.0/24
   ,smsk folu tlu cd,hl we;( 192.248.87.0/24
    Both are valid host IPs
    folu j,x.= i;aldrl IP fõ

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 3)
   Neither are in the 192.248.87.0/24 subnet
   folu 192.248.87.0/24 Wmcd,fha fkdue;'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 4)
   Different network (192.248.40.0 and 192.248.41.0)
   úúO cd,hka ^192.248.40.0 iy 192.248.41.0&

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 5)

   192.248.60.2 192.248.87.0/24 Wmcd,fha fkdue;

    Final Answer / wjidk ms<s;=r
    (2) 192.248.87.4, 192.248.87.5
    Because both addresses are in the same Class C subnet 192.248.87.0/24 and are valid for server allocation.
    ,smsk folu tlu C mka;sfha Wmcd,hl 192.248.87.0/24 jk w;r fiajdodhl fjka lsÍu i|yd j,x.=
    fõ'

 3. In communication networks, ISDN stands for
    ikaksfõok cd,j, § ISDN u.ska oelafjkafka

    1)   Integrated Service Domain Name.
    2)   Internet Service Directory Name.
    3)   Integrated Service Digital Network.
    4)   Internet Service Digital Network.
    5)   Integrated Service Domain Network.

                                                                                                 ( 2013 – 43 )
    Answer: 3)
    Correct Answer / ksjerÈ ms<s;=r
    (3) Integrated Service Digital Network

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    ISDN stands for Integrated Services Digital Network.
    ISDN hkq tAldnoaO fiajd äðg,a cd,hhs'

    It is a set of communication standards that allow the digital transmission of voice, video, data, and other
    network services over traditional telephone networks.
    th idïm%odhsl ÿrl:k cd, yryd yv" ùäfhda" o;a; iy wfkl=;a cd, fiajd äðg,a iïfm%aIKhg
    bv i,ik ikaksfõok m%ñ;Ska iuQyhls'

    Key Features / m%Odk úfYaIdx.
    Allows multiple digital channels on a single physical line.
    ;ks fN!;sl ud¾.hl nyq äðg,a kd,sld j,g bv i,ihs'

    Supports simultaneous voice and data transmission.
    tljr yv iy o;a; iïfm%aIKhg iydh fõ'

    Commonly used before widespread adoption of broadband internet.
    fn%daânEkaâ wka;¾cd,h mq¿,a f,i Ndú;d lsÍug fmr nyq,j Ndú;d fõ'

    OSI iuqoafoaY wdlD;sfha § cd,hl we;s mß.Kl folla w;r ikaksfõokfha § we;sjk jeros fidhd
    .ekqu (detect) ............. ld¾hhla jkafka h' by; ysia;ek msrùug jvd;a u fhda.H ms<s;=r l=ula o?

    1)   physical layer / fN!;sl ia:rfhys
    2)   data link layer / o;a; iïnkaOl ia:rfhys
    3)   network layer / cd, ia:rfhys
    4)   transport layer / m%jdyk ia:rfhys
    5)   application layer / fhÿï ia:rfhys

    Answer: 2)

    Correct Answer / ksjerÈ ms<s;=r
    (2) data link layer / o;a; iïnkaOl ia:rfhys

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    In the OSI reference model, the Data Link Layer (Layer 2) is responsible for
    OSI fhduq wdlD;sfha" o;a; ine¢ ia:rh ^2 jk ia:rh& j.lsj hq;af;a
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -24-                                          #ictfromabc


    •   Framing of data for transmission
        iïfm%aIKh i|yd o;a; rduq lsÍu'

    •   Error detection using methods like CRC (Cyclic Redundancy Check) or checksums.
        CRC ^pl%Sh w;sßla;;d mÍlaIdj& fyda fplaiï jeks l%u Ndú;fhka fodaI y÷kd.ekSu'

    •   Acknowledgment of frames and retransmission in case of errors (in protocols like HDLC or Ethernet
        with error detection).
        fodaI we;s úg rduq ms<s.ekSu iy kej; iïfm%aIKh ^HDLC fyda fodaI y÷kd.ekSu iys; B;¾fkÜ
        jeks fm%dfgdaflda,j,&'

    Key role: Ensures that data transmitted over physical link (e.g., Ethernet cable) is received without errors.
    m%Odk ld¾hNdrh( fN!;sl ine¢h yryd iïfm%aIKh jk o;a; ^Wod( B;¾fkÜ flan,h& fodaI fkdue;sj
    ,efnk nj iy;sl lrhs'

    Why the Other Layers Are Incorrect / wfkl=;a ia:r jerÈ jkafka wehs

    (1) Physical Layer / fN!;sl ia:rh
        Deals with raw bit transmission (voltages, cables).
        wuq ìÜ iïfm%aIKh ^fjda,aàh;d" flan,a& iuÕ lghq;= lrhs'
        Has no ability to detect or correct errors.

        In the OSI model, error detection during transmission is handled lower — by the data link layer.

        ia:rh u.ska'

        wjidk mßYS,l fhÿï iuÕ w;=reuqyqK;a'
        Not concerned with network transmission errors.

    Final Answer / wjidk ms<s;=r
    (2) data link layer / o;a; ine¢ ia:rh

        Data Link Layer in the OSI model.

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 4)
   A router connects multiple networks.
   uxyiqrejla nyq cd, iïnkaO lrhs'
    Operates at Layer 3 (Network layer).

    How to Get the Correct Answer
    The question asks which device can connect two different physical networks having IP addresses:
    m%Yakh wikafka IP ,smsk iys; úúO fN!;sl cd, 2la iïnkaO l< yelafla l=uk Wmdx.hgo hkakhs(

 2. A special digit inserted into a sequence of digits for data validation is called the ...............................
    digit. Which of the following is most appropriate to fill the blank in the above statement?
    o;a; im%udK;dj (data validation) i|yd ixLHdxl wkql%uhla ;=<g we;=<;a lrkq ,nk úfYaIs; jQ

 ➢ Option / úl,amh 2)

   ixLHdjl ,l=K (Ok fyda iDK) fj; fhduq fõ" j,x.=lrKhg wod< fkdfõ'

   l=vdu ia:dkSh w.h (ol=Kq fl<jfra) iys; b,lalï fj; fhduq fõ" j,x.=lrKhg wod< fkdfõ'

 6. Which of the following is a valid subnet mask?
    my; i|yka oE w;=frka j,x.= Wmcd, wdjrKhla (subnet mask) jkafka ljrla o?

     1) 255.255.255.192                 2) 255.0.255.0                  3) 256.255.255.64
     4) 255.256.255.96                  5) 0.0.0.255

                                                                                               ( 2014 – 28 )
     Answer: 1)

     Correct Answer / ksjerÈ ms<s;=r: 1) 255.255.255.192

     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
     A subnet mask is used in IP networking to divide the IP address into network and host portions.
     IP cd,lrKfha§ IP ,smskh cd, iy i;aldrl fldgia j,g fn§ug Wmcd, wdjrKhla Ndú;d lrhs'

     It must meet two conditions / th fldkafoais folla imqrd,sh hq;=h
     It must be a 32-bit number.
     th ìgq 32 wxlhla úh hq;=h'

     The binary form must contain a series of 1s followed by a series of 0s — with no mixing in between
     (e.g., 11111111.11111111.11111111.11000000 is valid).
     oaúuh wdlD;sfha 1s fY%aKshla iy 0s fY%aKshla wvx.= úh hq;=h - tAjd w;r ñY%Khla fkdue;sj
     ^Wod( 11111111.11111111.11111111.11000000 j,x.= fõ&'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                             -35-                                   #ictfromabc


    Check each option
    tla tla úl,amh mÍlaId lruq
 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 1)
   Valid / j,x.= fõ
   Binary: 11111111.11111111.11111111.11000000
    This is a valid subnet mask (CIDR /26).
    fuh j,x.= Wmcd, wdjrKhls (CIDR /26).

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 2)
   Invalid / j,x.= fkdfõ
   Binary: 11111111.00000000.11111111.00000000
    The 1s are not contiguous — breaks the rule of consecutive 1s followed by 0s.
    1s tlsfklg hdno fkdfõ - wkql%ñl 1s iy 0s hk Í;sh lv lrhs'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 3)
   Invalid / j,x.= fkdfõ
   256 is not a valid number in an IP address or subnet mask (range is 0–255).
   256 hkq IP ,smskhl fyda Wmcd, wdjrKhl j,x.= wxlhla fkdfõ ^mrdih 0–255&'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 4)
   Invalid / j,x.= fkdfõ
   256 is again out of range — invalid.
   256 kej;;a mrdifhka msg; - j,x.= fkdfõ'
 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 5)
   Invalid / j,x.= fkdfõ
   Binary: 00000000.00000000.00000000.11111111
    Starts with 0s, then 1s — reversed, which is not allowed in subnet masks.
    0s j,ska wdrïN fõ" miqj 1s - m%;sf,dau lr we;" th Wmcd, wdjrK j, wjir ke;'

    Therefore, option 1) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 1) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 7. The ping command indicates that there is a 5% packet loss between the computers X and Y. There is
    an FTP server running on Y. A file is downloaded to X from Y using FTP protocol. Which of the

    X yd Y mß.Kl w;r 5% l melÜgq ydkshla isÿj we;s nj ping úOdkh u.ska fmkajhs' Y
    mß.Klfha FTP fiajdodhlhla l%shd;aul fjñka mj;S' FTP kshudj,sh Ndú; lr f.dkqjla Y
    mß.Klfha isg X mß.Klh fj; nd.kq ,efí' my; i|yka j.ka;s w;=frka fuu f.dkq nd.;

    4) The downloaded file has the data in exactly the same order as the original file.

    5) FTP protocol cannot run on a network connection with errors.
       fodaI iys; cd, iïnkaOkhl FTP kshudj,sh Odjkh l< fkdyels h'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                          -36-                              #ictfromabc


    Answer: 4)

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 1)
   Inorrect / jerÈ
   The 5% packet loss shown by ping does not mean permanent data loss.
   ping uÕska fmkajk 5% melÜ w,dNh iaÓr o;a; w,dNhla woyia fkdfõ'

    FTP uses TCP, which can retransmit lost packets.
    FTP TCP Ndú;d lrhs" th ke;sjQ melÜ kej; iïfm%aIKh l< yelsh'

    So the file is not missing 5% — all data can still be delivered correctly.
    tneúka f.dkqj 5% la w;=reoyka ù ke; - ish¨u o;a; ;ju;a ksjerÈj ,nd Èh yelsh'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 2)
   Inorrect / jerÈ
   There is no reason to assume more data is missing than the packet loss shown by ping.
   ping uÕska fmkajk melÜ w,dNhg jvd jeä o;a; w;=reoyka ù we;ehs Wml,amkh lsÍug lsisÿ
   fya;=jla ke;'
    Again, TCP ensures reliable delivery by resending any lost packets.
    kej;;a" TCP ke;sjQ melÜ kej; heùfuka úYajdiodhl fnod yeÍu iy;sl lrhs'
    So, no data should be missing at all.
    tneúka" lsisÿ o;a;hla lsisfia;a w;=reoyka fkdúh hq;=h'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 3)
   Inorrect / jerÈ
   Even if packets arrive out of order, TCP reorders them before passing data to FTP.
   melÜ ms<sfj,g fkd;snqK;a" FTP fj; o;a; ,nd §ug fmr TCP tAjd kej; weKjqï lrhs'
    TCP guarantees in-order delivery.
    TCP wkqms<sfj,g fnod yeÍu iy;sl lrhs'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 4)
   Correct / ksjerÈ
   TCP ensures / TCP iy;sl lrhs
   • All data is received (via retransmission).
      ish¨u o;a; ,efnkq we; ^kej; iïfm%aIKh yryd&'
   • Correct order of data (via sequence numbers).
      o;a;j, ksjerÈ wkqms<sfj, ^wkql%ñl wxl yryd&'

    So, the file downloaded by FTP will be complete and in the correct order.
    tneúka" FTP u.ska nd.; l< f.dkqj iïmQ¾K iy ksjerÈ wkqms<sfj,ska jkq we;'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 5)
   Inorrect / jerÈ
   FTP can still run successfully over connections with packet loss.
   melÜ w,dNh iuÕ iïnkaO;d yryd FTP ;ju;a id¾:lj l%shd;aul úh yel'

    This is because TCP (which FTP relies on) handles
    fuhg fya;=j TCP ^FTP r|d mj;sk& yiqrejkafka
    • Packet loss / melÜ w,dNh
    • Retransmission / kej; iïfm%aIKh
    • Flow and congestion control / m%jdyh iy ;onoh md,kh

    How Do You Get the Correct Answer?

    fmkakqï lrhs'

        l< yelsh'

    Correct Answer: (4)
    Because / ulaksido h;a

    TCP ensures reliable, in-order delivery of data.
    TCP úYajdiodhl" ms<sfj,g o;a; fnod yeÍu iy;sl lrhs'

             2015 AL NETWORKING MCQ MARKING

   1. ............................ is used for analog signal to digital signal conversion.
      m%;siu ix{djla ixLHdxl ix{djla njg mßj¾;kh lsÍug ....................... Ndú; lrkq ,efí'
          Which of the following is most appropriate to fill the blank in the above statement?
          by; j.ka;sfha ysia;ek msrùug jvd;a u fhda.H jkafka my; i|yka ljrla o?
          1)    Amplitude Modulation / úia;dr uQ¾ckh (AM)
          2)    Frequency Modulation / ixLHd; uQ¾ckh (FM)
          3)    Pulse Code Modulation / iamkaÈ; fla; uQ¾ckh (PCM)
          4)    Phase Modulation / l,d uQ¾ckh (PM)
          5)    Time Division Modulation / ld, fnÿï uQ¾ckh (AM)

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2015 - 13 )
          Answer: 3)

          Modulation is the process of varying one or more properties (such as its amplitude, frequency, or phase)
          of periodic waveform called the carrier signal to encode information for transmission over a
          communication channel.
          uqyq¾cKh hkq ikaksfõok kd,sldjla yryd iïfm%aIKh i|yd f;dr;=re fla;kh lsÍug jdyl ix{dj
          f,iska ye¢kafjk wdj¾;sl ;rx. wdlD;shl .=Kdx. tlla fyda lsysmhla ^tys úia;drh" ixLHd;h
          fyda l,dj jeks& fjkia lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs'

          Amplitude Modulation (AM) / úia;dr uQ¾ckh
          In amplitude modulation, the amplitude of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
          information (baseband) signal, while the frequency and phase of the carrier remain constant.
          úia;drh uqyq¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ úia;drh f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia
          lrk w;r" jdylfha ixLHd;h iy l,dj ksh;j mj;S'

          In AM, the louder the signal (for example, a voice or music), the higher the amplitude of the carrier wave,
          and the quieter the signal, the lower the amplitude.
          AM j,§" ix{dj ^WodyrKhla f,i" yvla fyda ix.S;hla& jeä jk ;rug" jdyl ;rx.fha úia;drh
          jeä jk w;r" ix{dj ksYaYío jk ;rug" úia;drh wvq fõ'

          Frequency modulation (FM) / ixLHd; uQ¾ckh
          In frequency modulation, the frequency of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
          information (baseband) signal, while the amplitude and phase of the carrier remain constant.
          ixLHd; uqyq¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ ixLHd;h f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia
          lrk w;r" jdylfha úia;drh iy l,dj ksh;j mj;S'

          The louder the information signal, the more the frequency of the carrier signal shifts.
          f;dr;=re ix{dfõ Yíoh jeä jk ;rug jdyl ix{dfõ ixLHd;h jeä fõ'

          Phase modulation (PM) / l,d uQ¾ckh
          In phase modulation, the phase of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
          information (baseband) signal, while the carrier’s amplitude and frequency remain constant.
          l,d uQ¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ l,dj f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia lrk w;r"
          jdylfha úia;drh iy ixLHd;h ksh;j mj;S'

          As the amplitude of the information signal changes, the phase of the carrier shifts accordingly.
          f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drh fjkia jk úg" jdylfha l,dj tA wkqj fjkia fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -39-                                           #ictfromabc



    Pulse Code Modulation (PCM) / iamkaos; fla; uQ¾ckh
    Pulse Code Modulation encoding is a technique used to convert analog signals into digital signals.
    iamkaos; fla; uQ¾ckh hkq m%;siu ix{d wxls; ix{d njg mßj¾;kh lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk
    ;dlaIKhls'

    Time Division Modulation (TDM) / ld, fnÿï uQ¾ckh
    Time Division Modulation (TDM) is a technique used in communication systems to transmit multiple
    signals over a single communication channel by dividing the time into several slots. Each signal is assigned
    a specific time slot, and the signals take turns using the channel.
    ld, fnÿï uQ¾ckh hkq ikaksfõok moaO;sj, Ndú;d lrk ;dlaIKhls" th ld,h ;õ lsysmhlg
    fn§fuka ;ks ikaksfõok kd,sldjla yryd nyq ix{d iïfm%aIKh lrhs' iEu ix{djlgu ksYaÑ; ld,
    mr;rhla mjrd we;s w;r" ix{d udrefjka udrejg kd,sldj Ndú;d lrhs'

    Correct Answer / ksjerÈ ms<s;=r
    3) Pulse Code Modulation (PCM) / iamkaos; fla; uQ¾ckh

    Why PCM is correct / PCM ksjerÈ jkafka wehs
    Pulse Code Modulation (PCM) is a technique that converts analog signals (continuous waveforms) into
    digital signals (binary data). It is widely used in digital audio systems, telephone networks, and other
    communication systems. / iamkaos; fla; uQ¾ckh hkq m%;siu ix{d ^wLKav ;rx. wdldr& äðg,a
    ix{d ^oaúuh o;a;& njg mßj¾;kh lrk ;dlaIKhls' th äðg,a Y%jH moaO;s" ÿrl:k cd, iy
    wfkl=;a ikaksfõok moaO;sj, nyq,j Ndú;d fõ'

    PCM works in 3 main steps / PCM m%Odk mshjr 3 lska l%shd lrhs
    Sampling – The analog signal is sampled at regular time intervals.
    kshe§u - m%;siu ix{dj ks;sm;d ld, mr;rhkays§ idïm, ,nd .kS'
    Quantization – Each sample is approximated to the nearest value from a finite set of levels.
    m%udKlrKh - iEu ksheÈhlau iSñ; uÜgï iuQyhlska wdikak;u w.hg wdikak fõ'
    Encoding – Each quantized value is encoded into a binary number.
    fla;kh lsÍu - iEu m%udKd;aul w.hlau oaúuh wxlhlg fla;kh lr we;'
    Result: The continuous analog waveform becomes a stream of binary digits (digital signal).
    m%;sM,h( wLKav m%;siu ;rx. wdlD;sh oaúuh b,lalï m%jdyhla njg m;afõ ^äðg,a ix{d&'

   Wmcd, wdjrKh (/25) f;areï .kak

    Wmcd, wdjrKh /25 (255.255.255.128) hkafkka woyia jkafka

    m<uq ìgq 25 cd,h i|yd fõ

 4. A LAN uses the subnet mask 255.255.240.0. How many different IP addresses can be assigned to
    devices in this LAN?
    ia:dkSh fmfoia cd,hla (LAN) 255.255.240.0 hk Wmcd, wdjrKh (subnet mask) Ndú; lrhs' fuu
    cd,fha mj;sk Wml%u i|yd Ndú; l< yels tlsfklg fjkia IP ,smsk fldmuK ixLHdjla mj;skafkao?
    1) 254                   2) 256               3) 1024           4) 2046                 5) 4094

                                                                                               ( 2015 - 19 )
    Answer: 5)

    To solve this, we need to calculate the number of assignable IP addresses based on the given subnet mask
    fuh úi£u i|yd" ,nd § we;s Wmcd, wdjrKh u; mokïj mejßh yels IP ,smsk .Kk .Kkh
    l< hq;=h

    Subnet Mask / Wmcd, wdjrKh : 255.255.240.0

 ➢ Step 1
   Convert the subnet mask to CIDR (/x) format
   Wmcd, wdjrKh CIDR (/x) wdlD;shg mßj¾;kh lrkak

    Convert 255.255.240.0 to binary
    255.255.240.0 oaúuh njg mßj¾;kh lrkak

    255    = 11111111
    255    = 11111111
    240    = 11110000
    0      = 00000000

    This gives / fuh ,nd fokafka
    11111111.11111111.11110000.00000000 → total 20 bits for the network.
    11111111.11111111.11110000.00000000 → cd,h i|yd uq¿ ìgq 20.

    So this is /20 subnet mask
    Tneúka fuh /20 Wmcd, wdjrKhls

 ➢ Step 2
   Calculate number of host addresses
   i;aldrl ,smsk .Kk .Kkh lrkak

    Total bits = 32
    Host bits = 32 - 20 = 12 bits
    Number of addresses = 212 = 4096

    But 2 addresses are reserved / kuq;a ,smsk 2la fjka lr we;

    1 for the network address / cd, ,smskh i|yd 1
    1 for the broadcast address / úldYk ,smskh i|yd 1

    Usable addresses = 4096 – 2 = 4094
    Ndú;d l< yels ,smsk = 4096 – 2 = 4094

    Therefore, option 5) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 5) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 6. In a public key encryption system, the private key of a person x is given by the function priv(x) and

       priv(x) Ndú; lsÍu u.ska muKla úfla;kh (decrypt) l< yels fia mKsjqvhla .=ma; fla;kh
       lsÍu i|yd pub(x) Ndú; lrhs'
    B. pub(x) is used to sign a message to be sent to x.
       x fj; hjk mKsjqvhl w;aika ;eîu (sign) i|yd pub(x) Ndú; lrhs'
    C. A message encrypted using pub(x) can be decrypted using pub(x).
       pub(x) Ndú;fhka .=ma; fla;kh lrkq ,enQ mKsjqvhla pub(x) Ndú;fhka úfla;kh l< yels fõ'
    Which of the above statement(s) is/are correct?
    by; j.ka;s w;=frka ksjerÈ jkafka"

    1) A only.          2) B only.          3) C only.      4) A and B only.     5) B and C only.
       A muKs.             B muKs.             C muKs.         A yd B muKs.         B yd C muKs.

    Answer: 1)

    In public key cryptography / fmdÿ h;=re .=ma; fla;kfha§

    Each person x has:
    iEu mqoa.,fhl=gu x we;af;a

    •   A public key: pub(x) (shared with everyone)
        fmdÿ h;=rla( pub(x) ^ish¨ fokd iuÕ fnodf.k we;&

    •   A private key: priv(x) (kept secret)
        fm!oa.,sl h;=rla( priv(x) ^ryis.;j ;nd we;&

    There are two main uses of public key cryptography:
    fmdÿ h;=re .=ma; fla;kfha§ m%Odk Ndú;hka folla ;sfí(

    Confidentiality (Encryption) / ryiHNdjh ^fla;kh&
    Encrypt with recipient's public key → only recipient’s private key can decrypt.
    ,nkakdf.a fmdÿ h;=r iuÕ fla;kh lrkak → ,nkakdf.a mqoa.,sl h;=rg muKla úfla;kh l<
    yelsh'
    Ensures only the intended recipient can read the message.
    wfmalaIs; ,nkakdg muKla mKsúvh lshúh yels nj iy;sl lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -47-                                    #ictfromabc


    Authentication / Digital Signatures / i;Hdmkh $ äðg,a w;aika
    Sign with sender's private key → anyone can verify it using sender’s public key.
    hjkakdf.a mqoa.,sl h;=r iuÕ w;aika lrkak → ´kEu flfkl=g hjkakdf.a fmdÿ h;=r Ndú;fhka
    th i;Hdmkh l< yelsh'

    Ensures authenticity and integrity.
    i;H;dj iy wLKav;dj iy;sl lrhs'

    Statement Analysis / m%ldYk úYaf,aIKh

 ➢ Statement / m%ldYh A)

    Correct / ksjerÈ

    This is the standard use for ensuring confidentiality.
    ryiHNdjh iy;sl lsÍu i|yd iïu; Ndú;h fuhhs'
    Encrypt with recipient’s public key → only they (with priv(x)) can decrypt.
    ,nkakdf.a fmdÿ h;=r iuÕ fla;kh lrkak → Tjqkag ^priv(x) iuÕ& muKla fla;kh l< yelsh'

 ➢ Statement / m%ldYh B)

    Inorrect / jerÈ

    Signing is done with the sender’s private key, not the recipient’s public key.
    w;aika lsÍu ,nkakdf.a fmdÿ h;=frka fkdj" hjkakdf.a mqoa.,sl h;=frka isÿ flf¾'
    You never "sign a message with pub(x)".
    Tn lsis úfgl;a ¶pub(x) iuÕ mKsúvhla w;aika lrkak¶ ke;'

 ➢ Statement / m%ldYh C)

    Inorrect / jerÈ

    Only the corresponding private key priv(x) can decrypt what was encrypted with pub(x).
    Pub(x) iuÕ fla;kh l< foa úfla;kh l< yelafla wkqrEm mqoa.,sl h;=r priv(x) g muKs'

    Therefore, option 1) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 1) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

     Answer: 1)

     Let’s analyze each statement about the World Wide Web (WWW)
     f,dal jHdma; úhuk WWW ms<sn| tla tla m%ldYh úYaf,aIKh lruq

 ➢ Statement / m%ldYh A)
   Correct. This is the proper definition of the World Wide Web. It consists of websites made up of web pages
   that are interlinked and accessed through browsers using the Internet.
   ksjerÈhs' fuh f,dal jHdma; fjí wvúfha ksis w¾: oelaùuhs' th wka;¾ iïnkaê; iy wka;¾cd,h
   Ndú;fhka n%õi¾ yryd m%fõY jk fjí msgq j,ska ieÿï,;a fjí wvú j,ska iukaú; fõ'

   distribute information via the web. The WWW is a service that runs on top of the Internet, using HTTP as
   a protocol.
   jerÈhs' WWW hkq fm%dfgdaflda,hla fkdfõ' HTTP hkq fjnh yryd f;dr;=re fnod yeÍu i|yd
   Ndú;d lrk fm%dfgdaflda,hhs' WWW hkq HTTP fm%dfgdaflda,hla f,i Ndú;d lrñka wka;¾cd,hg
   by<ska l%shd;aul jk fiajdjls'

   Incorrect. The WWW was invented by Tim Berners-Lee in 1989 while working at CERN. The W3C
   (World Wide Web Consortium) was later formed (in 1994) to standardize web technologies — it did not
   invent the Web.
   jerÈhs' WWW, 1989 § CERN ys fiajh lrñka isáh§ áï n¾k¾ia ,S úiska fidhd .kakd ,§' W3C

   - th fjnh ks¾udKh lf<a ke;'

 9. A file of 1 MB has been successfully sent from the machine X to machine Y in a network over a TCP
    connection. It has been observed that the 10th byte of the file has passed through the router R.
    Consider the following statements regarding this communication:
    cd,hl X kï hka;%hl isg Y kï hka;%hla fj; 1MB jQ f.dkqjla TCP iïnkaOhla Nryd id¾:lj
    hjk ,§' fuu f.dkqfõ 10 jeks nhsgh R kï jQ uxyiqrej (router) ;=<ska .uka lr we;s nj
    ksßlaIKh úh' fuu ikaksfõokh iïnkaOfhka my; § we;s j.ka;s i,ld n,kak(

    A. The 10,000th byte must have gone through the router R after the 10th byte.
       10 jeks nhsghg miq 10,000 jeks nhsgh o" R kï jQ uxyiqrej ;=<ska .uka lr ;sìh hq;=u h'
    B. The 10,000th byte must have gone through the same path from X to Y as the 10th byte.
       10,000 jeks nhsgh o X isg Y olajd 10 jeks nhsgh .uka .;a ud¾.fha u .uka lr ;sìh
       hq;=u h'
    C. The 10,000th byte may or may not have gone through the router R.
       10,000 jeks nhsgh R kï jQ uxyiqrej ;=<ska .uka lr fyda .uka fkdlr fyda ;sìh yels h'

    Which of the above statement(s) is/are correct?
    by; i|yka l=uk j.ka;sh$ j.ka;s ksjerÈ jkafka o?

    1) A only.         2) B only.          3) C only.          4) A and B only.      5) B and C only.
       A muKs.            B muKs.             C muKs.             A yd B muKs.          B yd C muKs.

                                                                                                 ( 2015 - 43 )
    Answer: 3)

    Let’s analyze each statement based on how TCP and IP routing work.
    TCP iy IP rjqáka l%shd lrk wdldrh u; mokïj wms tla tla m%ldYh úYaf,aIKh lruq'

    Context / ikao¾Nh
    File size / f.dkq m%udKh: 1 MB (≈ 1,048,576 bytes)

    The file is sent from machine X to machine Y over TCP.
    f.dkqj hka;% X isg hka;% Y olajd TCP yryd hjkq ,efí'

    The 10th byte is known to have passed through router R.
    10 jk nhsgh R rjqgrh yryd .uka lr we;s nj okakd lreKls'

    Asked about the 10,000th byte.
    10,000 jk nhsgh .ek úuikq ,efí'

    Statement Analysis / m%ldYk úYaf,aIKh

 ➢ Statement / m%ldYh A)
    Inorrect / jerÈ
    TCP ensures in-order delivery at the destination, but IP (underneath TCP) is connectionless and stateless.
    TCP .ukdka;fha§ wkqms<sfj,g fnod yeÍu iy;sl lrhs" kuq;a IP ^TCP hgf;a& iïnkaO;d rys;
    iy ;;ajhlska f;drh'

    IP routing can change per packet (due to load balancing, dynamic routing, etc.).
    melÜgqjlg IP rjqáka fjkia úh yelsh ^nr ;=,kh" .;sl ud¾..; lsÍu wdÈh fya;=fjka&'

    So, it's not guaranteed that the 10,000th byte went through the same router R, even if the 10th byte did.
    tneúka" 10 jk nhsgh tfia l<;a" 10,000 jk nhsgh tlu R rjqgrh yryd .sh nj iy;sl fkdfõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                             -51-                                       #ictfromabc



 ➢ Statement / m%ldYh B)
    Inorrect / jerÈ
    Similar to above: IP does not guarantee that packets (even belonging to the same TCP connection) take
    the same path.
    by; i|yka l< wdldrhgu( melÜ ^tlu TCP iïnkaO;djhg wh;a jqjo& tlu ud¾.h .kakd njg
    IP iy;sl fkdfõ'

    Routing decisions may change mid-connection, especially in large networks or the internet.
    úfYaIfhka úYd, cd, fyda wka;¾cd,h ;=< ud¾..; lsÍfï ;SrK iïnkaO;djh ueo fjkia l<
    yelsh'

 ➢ Statement / m%ldYh C)
    Correct / ksjerÈ
    This statement is accurate.
    fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs'

    Due to dynamic routing, the 10,000th byte might have gone through R — or it might not have.
    .;sl ud¾..; lsÍu ksid" 10,000 jk nhsgh R yryd f.dia ;sìh yelsh - ke;fyd;a th fkd;sìh
    yelsh'

    There's no certainty unless network enforces path consistency (e.g., MPLS), which is not assumed here.
    cd,h ud¾. wkql+,;dj ^Wod( MPLS& l%shd;aul lrk f;la ksYaÑ;Ndjhla fkdue;" th fuys
    Wml,amkh fkdflf¾'

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

             2016 AL NETWORKING MCQ MARKING

   1. “An analog signal is sampled at regular intervals and represented as 16 bit values.”
      "m%;siu ix{djla ksh; ld,dka;rj, oS ksheÈ lr ìgq 16 ys w.hka f,i ksrEmKh lrkq ,efí'"
          Which of the followings is best described by the above statement?
          by; j.ka;sh fyd¢ka u úia;r lrkq ,nkafka my; i|yka l=ulska o?

          1)    Amplitude Modulation / úia;dr uQ¾ckh (AM)
          2)    Frequency Modulation / ixLHd; uQ¾ckh (FM)
          3)    Pulse Code Modulation / iamkaÈ; fla; uQ¾ckh (PCM)
          4)    Phase Modulation / l,d uQ¾ckh (PM)
          5)    Pulse Width Modulation / iamkao ú;r uQ¾ckh (PWM)
                                                                                                                                                         ( 2016 - 12 )
          Answer : 3 )

          Modulation is the process of varying one or more properties (such as its amplitude, frequency, or phase)
          of periodic waveform called the carrier signal to encode information for transmission over a
          communication channel.
          uqyq¾cKh hkq ikaksfõok kd,sldjla yryd iïfm%aIKh i|yd f;dr;=re fla;kh lsÍug jdyl ix{dj
          f,iska ye¢kafjk wdj¾;sl ;rx. wdlD;shl .=Kdx. tlla fyda lsysmhla ^tys úia;drh" ixLHd;h
          fyda l,dj jeks& fjkia lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs'

          Amplitude Modulation (AM) / úia;dr uQ¾ckh
          In amplitude modulation, the amplitude of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
          information (baseband) signal, while the frequency and phase of the carrier remain constant.
          úia;drh uqyq¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ úia;drh f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia
          lrk w;r" jdylfha ixLHd;h iy l,dj ksh;j mj;S'

          In AM, the louder the signal (for example, a voice or music), the higher the amplitude of the carrier wave,
          and the quieter the signal, the lower the amplitude.
          AM j,§" ix{dj ^WodyrKhla f,i" yvla fyda ix.S;hla& jeä jk ;rug" jdyl ;rx.fha úia;drh
          jeä jk w;r" ix{dj ksYaYío jk ;rug" úia;drh wvq fõ'

          Frequency modulation (FM) / ixLHd; uQ¾ckh
          In frequency modulation, the frequency of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
          information (baseband) signal, while the amplitude and phase of the carrier remain constant.
          ixLHd; uqyq¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ ixLHd;h f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia
          lrk w;r" jdylfha úia;drh iy l,dj ksh;j mj;S'

          The louder the information signal, the more the frequency of the carrier signal shifts.
          f;dr;=re ix{dfõ Yíoh jeä jk ;rug jdyl ix{dfõ ixLHd;h jeä fõ'

          Phase modulation (PM) / l,d uQ¾ckh
          In phase modulation, the phase of the carrier signal is varied in proportion to the amplitude of the
          information (baseband) signal, while the carrier’s amplitude and frequency remain constant.
          l,d uQ¾ckfha§" jdyl ix{dfõ l,dj f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drhg iudkqmd;slj fjkia lrk w;r"
          jdylfha úia;drh iy ixLHd;h ksh;j mj;S'

          As the amplitude of the information signal changes, the phase of the carrier shifts accordingly.
          f;dr;=re ix{dfõ úia;drh fjkia jk úg" jdylfha l,dj tA wkqj fjkia fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -51-                                          #ictfromabc



    Pulse Code Modulation (PCM) / iamkaok fla; uQ¾ckh
    Pulse Code Modulation encoding is a technique used to convert analog signals into digital signals.
    iamkaok fla; uQ¾ckh hkq m%;siu ix{d wxls; ix{d njg mßj¾;kh lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk
    ;dlaIKhls'

    Pulse Width Modulation (PWM) / iamkao ú;r uQ¾ckh
    Pulse Width Modulation (PWM) is a technique used to control the power delivered to electrical devices
    by varying the width (duration) of digital pulses. / iamkao ú;r uQ¾ckh hkq äðg,a iamkaokj, m<,
    ^ld,iSudj& fjkia lsÍfuka úoHq;a Wmdx.j,g ,nd fok n,h md,kh lsÍug Ndú;d lrk ;dlaIKhls'

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    The statement "An analog signal is sampled at regular intervals and represented as 16-bit values" perfectly
    describes Pulse Code Modulation (PCM) / m%;siu ix{djla ks;sm;d ld, mr;rhkays§ idïm, ,ndf.k
    ìgq 16 w.hka f,i ksrEmKh flf¾ hk m%ldYh iamkaok fla; uQ¾ckh mßmQ¾K f,i úia;r lrhs
    Sampling: The analog signal is measured at regular time intervals.
    kshe§u( m%;siu ix{dj ks;sm;d ld, mr;rhkays§ ukskq ,efí'
    Quantization: The amplitude of each sample is assigned a discrete value (e.g., using 16-bit binary).
    m%udKkh( tla tla idïm,fha úia;drhg úúla; w.hla mjrkq ,efí ^Wod( ìgq 16 oaúuh Ndú;d lsÍu&'
    Encoding: These values are converted into a binary format for digital transmission or storage.
    fla;kh( fuu w.hka äðg,a iïfm%aIKh fyda .nvd lsÍug oaúuh wdlD;shla njg mßj¾;kh fõ'
    This is exactly how PCM works — it's a digital representation of an analog signal.
    PCM l%shd lrk wdldrh fuhhs - th m%;siu ix{djl äðg,a ksrEmKhls'

    Why the other options are incorrect / wfkl=;a úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 1)
    odifies the amplitude of a carrier wave in response to the analog signal.
    m%;siu ix{djg m%;spdr jYfhka jdyl ;rx.hl úia;drh fjkia lrhs'
    Does not involve sampling or digitization.
    kshe§u fyda äðg,alrKh we;=<;a fkdfõ'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 2)
    Modulates the frequency of a carrier signal.
    jdyl ix{djl ixLHd;h uQ¾ckh lrhs'
    Again, it's analog and doesn't use 16-bit samples.
    kej;;a" th m%;siu jk w;r ìgq 16 idïm, Ndú;d fkdlrhs'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Changes the phase of the carrier wave in accordance with the signal.
    ix{djg wkql+,j jdyl ;rx.fha wÈhr fjkia lrhs'
    Also analog, not digital sampling.
    ;jo äðg,a idïm,lrKh fkdj m%;siuh'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 5)
    Varies the width of pulses to represent signal amplitude.
    ix{d úia;drh ksrEmKh lsÍu i|yd iamkaokj, m<, fjkia lrhs'
    It's used more in power control and not equivalent to PCM’s 16-bit sampling method.
    th n, md,kfha§ jeämqr Ndú;d jk w;r PCM ys ìgq 16 idïm,lrK l%uhg iudk fkdfõ'

 6. Two machines with the IP addresses 192.248.16.30 and 192.248.16.90 are connected to a Local Area
    Network (LAN). Which of the followings is a suitable subnet mask for this network?
    IP ,smsk 192.248.16.30 iy 192.248.16.90 iys; hka;% folla ia:dkSh fmfoia cd,hlg (LAN)
    iïnkaO fldg we;' my; i|yka ljrla fuu cd,h i|yd iqÿiq Wmcd, wdjrKhla jkafka o?

    1) 192.255.255.255                2) 192.248.16.0                           3) 255.255.255.224
    4) 255.255.255.128                5) 255.255.255.255

                                                                                                    ( 2016 - 13 )
    Answer: 4)

    To determine a suitable subnet mask, we must make sure both IP addresses are in the same subnet.
    iqÿiq Wmcd, wdjrKhla ;SrKh lsÍu i|yd" wms IP ,smsk folu tlu Wmcd,hl we;s njg j. n,d
    .; hq;=h'

    Given / ,nd § we;
    IP1: 192.248.16.30
    IP2: 192.248.16.90

    Let’s examine the binary forms of the last octet of each IP (since the first 3 octets are the same)
    tla tla IP ys wjidk wIaGufha oaúuh wdldr mÍlaId lruq ^m<uq wIaGu 3 iudk neúka&

    192.248.16.30 → Last octet in binary / oaúuh ;=< wjidk wIaGuh : 00011110
    192.248.16.90 → Last octet in binary / oaúuh ;=< wjidk wIaGuh : 01011010

    We need a subnet mask that groups both of these addresses into the same network.
    fuu ,smsk folu tlu cd,hlg ldKav lrk Wmcd, wdjrKhla wmg wjYH fõ'

    Option (4): 255.255.255.128

    This is a /25 subnet mask, which means:
    fuh /25 Wmcd, wdjrKhla" tkï(

    Network size = 2^7 = 128 IP addresses
    cd, m%udKh = 2^7 = 128 IP ,smsk

    Valid range: 192.248.16.0 to 192.248.16.127
    j,x.= mrdih( 192.248.16.0 isg 192.248.16.127 olajd

    Check if both IPs fall into this range
    IP folu fuu mrdihg jefÜoehs mÍlaId lruq

    192.248.16.30 within range / mrdih ;=<
    192.248.16.90 within range / mrdih ;=<

    So both are in the same subnet.
    tneúka folu tlu Wmcd,fha we;'

    Why other options are wrong / fjk;a úl,am jerÈ jkafka wehs

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 1)
   Not a valid subnet mask (starts with 192 – invalid for a mask).
   j,x.= Wmcd, wdjrKhla fkdfõ ^192 ka wdrïN fõ - wdjrKhla i|yd j,x.= fkdfõ&'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 2)

   th cd, ,smskhls" Wmcd, wdjrKhla fkdfõ'

 ➢ Option/ úl,amh 3)
   /27 → Range: 192.248.16.0 – 192.248.16.31

 7. Which of the following is a correct IPv4 address?

    Answer: 3)

    IP addresses
    An IP address is a logical identifier assigned to a device when it connects to a network.
    IP ,smskhla hkq cd,hlg iïnkaO jk úg Wmdx.hlg mjrk ;d¾lsl y÷kd.ekSuls'

    It identifies the device at a higher level, such as within a larger network or the internet.
    th úYd, cd,hla fyda wka;¾cd,h jeks by< uÜgul§ Wmdx.h y÷kd .kS'

    Unlike MAC addresses, which are tied to hardware, IP addresses are logical and can change.
    oDVdx. iuÕ ne£ we;s MAC ,smsk fuka fkdj" IP ,smsk ;d¾lsl jk w;r fjkia úh yel'

    For instance, a device’s IP address may change if it connects to a different network.
    WodyrKhla f,i" Wmdx.hla fjkia cd,hlg iïnkaO jqjfyd;a tys IP ,smskh fjkia úh yel'

    Types of IP Addresses / IP ,smsk j¾.

    •   IPv4
        It uses 32-bit number format,represented as four decimal numbers separated by dots (Ex. 192.168.1.1).
        th ìgq 32 ixLHd wdlD;shla Ndú;d lrhs" ;s;a j,ska fjka lrk ,o oYu ixLHd y;rla f,i
        ksrEmKh flf¾ ^Wod' 192.168.1.1&'

        This is called the dotted decimal notation.
        fuh ;s;a oYuh wxlkh f,i ye¢kafõ'

    •   IPv6
        This newer version uses a 128-bit address format, represented as eight groups of four hexadecimal
        digits separated by colons.
        fuu kj wkqjdoh ìgq 128ys ,smsk wdlD;shla Ndú;d lrhs" flda,ka j,ska fjka lrk ,o IâoYuh
        b,lalï y;rl ldKav wgla f,i ksrEmKh flf¾'
        2001:0db8:85a3:0000:0000:8a2e:0370:7334
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -54-                                          #ictfromabc


     Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
     An IPv4 address consists of four octets, each separated by a dot (.), and each octet must be a number from
     0 to 255.
     IPv4 ,smskhla ;s;lska (.) fjka lrk ,o wIagl y;rlska iukaú; jk w;r" iEu wIaglhlau 0 isg
     255 olajd wxlhla úh hq;=h'

     Let’s examine each option,
     tla tla úl,amh mÍlaId lr n,uq"

     1) 192.248.0.0.1
        Incorrect: This has 5 octets (too many). IPv4 must have only 4 parts.
        jerÈhs( fuhg wIaGu 5la we; ^b;d jeäh&' IPv4 ys fldgia 4la muKla ;sìh hq;=h'

     2) 192.258.2.1
        Incorrect: The second octet is 258, which is greater than 255.
        jerÈhs( fojk wIaGuh 258 jk w;r th 255 g jvd jeäh'

     3) 8.8.8.8
        Correct: All four octets are valid numbers between 0 and 255.
        ksjerÈhs( ish¨u wIaGu y;ru 0 iy 255 w;r j,x.= ixLHd fõ'

         This is a well-known public DNS server provided by Google.
         fuh .+.,a úiska imhk ,o m%isoaO fmdÿ DNS fiajdodhlhls'

     4) 10.256.8.9
        Incorrect: The second octet is 256, which is outside the valid range.
        jerÈhs( fojk wIaGuh 256 jk w;r th j,x.= mrdifhka msg; fõ'

     5) 255.255.255.268
        Incorrect: The last octet is 268, which is more than 255.
        jerÈhs( wjidk wIaGuh 268 jk w;r th 255 g jvd jeäh'

     Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.

     OSI Model
     The OSI model, also known as the Open Systems Interconnection model, serves as a comprehensive and
     detailed conceptual framework designed to facilitate and enhance the understanding and implementation
     of network communications among computing systems.
     OSI wdlD;sh" újD; moaO;s wka;¾ iïnkaO;d wdlD;sh f,io ye¢kafõ" mß.Kl moaO;s w;r cd,
     ikaksfõokhka wjfndaO lr .ekSu iy l%shd;aul lsÍu myiq lsÍu iy jeäÈhqKq lsÍu i|yd ks¾udKh
     lr we;s úia;S¾K iy iúia;rd;aul ixl,amSh rduqjla f,i fiajh lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -55-                                           #ictfromabc


    Application layer / fhÿï ia:rh

    •   This is the layer where the user interacts.
        mßYS,lhd lghq;= lrk ia:rh fõ'
    •   Here, a networking environment is created using application software.
        fuys§ fhÿï uDÿldx. Ndú;fhka cd,lrK jgmsgdjla ks¾udkh lr fohs'
    •   Serves as the window for users and application processes to access network services.
        cd, fiajd fj; m%fõY ùug mßYS,lhska iy fhÿï l%shdj,Ska i|yd ljq¿j f,i l%shd lrhs'

    Presentation layer / iu¾mK ia:rh

    •   Here, Data is transformed between the application layer and the network.
        fuys§" o;a; fhÿï ia:rh yd cd,h w;r mßj¾;kh lsÍu isÿ lrhs'
    •   In order to ensure the security of data while sending and receiving, encryption and decryption of data
        are done in this layer. / o;a; heùfï§ tAjdfha wdrlaIdj ;yjqre lr .ekSu Wfoid ixfla;lrkh;a"
        o;a; ,eîfïÈ tAjd úixflalrkh;a isÿ lrkq ,nkafka fuu ia:rh ;=,§ fõ'
    •   As the data is connected to the Internet in the lower layers, i.e. from the transport layer down,
        encryption is done to prevent possible damage.
        o;a; my< ia:rj, wka;¾cd,hg iïnkaO lr we;s neúka" tkï m%jdyk ia:rfha isg my<g"
        isÿúh yels ydksh je<elaùu i|yd ixfla;kh lsÍu isÿ flf¾'

    Session layer / ieis ia:rh

    •   In communication that takes place in networking, it is essential that there is a better mutual relationship
        between the parties in which it takes place. The reason is that otherwise the data communicated can
        be communicated to someone else. / cd,lrKfha§ isÿjk ikaksfõokhla ;=< th isÿjk md¾Yaj
        w;r jvd fyd| wfkHdakH iïnkaOhla mej;Su w;HjYH fõ' Bg fya;=j tfia fkdjqjfyd;a
        ikaksfõokh lrkq ,nk o;a;h fjk;a whl=g jqjo ikaksfõokh ùug yels ùuhs'

    •   The OSI model’s session layer is responsible for managing and controlling conversations (or sessions)
        between applications. / OSI wdlD;sfha ieis ia:rh fhÿï w;r ixjdo ^fyda ieis& l<ukdlrKh
        lsÍu iy md,kh lsÍu i|yd j.lsj hq;=h

    Transport layer / m%jdyk ia:rh

    •   The transport layer's primary responsibility is to provide end-to-end communication between
        applications running on different hosts. / m%jdyk ia:rfha uQ,sl j.lSu jkafka úúO Odrl u;
        l%shd;aul jk fhÿï w;r wjidkh isg wjidkh olajd ikaksfõokh iemhSuhs'
    •   It provides mechanisms for error correction, flow control, and data integrity, making sure that data
        reaches its destination correctly and efficiently. / th fodaI ksjerÈ lsÍu" m%jdy md,kh iy o;a;
        wLKav;dj i|yd hdka;%K imhhs" o;a; ksjerÈj yd ld¾hlaIuj tys .ukdka;hg <Õd jk njg
        j. n,d .kS'

    •   Segmentation and reassembly of data is also done by this layer.
        o;a; LKavkh iy kej; tl,ia lsÍu isÿ lrkq ,nkafkao fuu ia:rh u.ska fõ'

    Network layer / cd, ia:rh

    •   This layer carries a data packet along a network to its destination. The IP address is used for that.
        o;a; melÜgqjla .ukdka;hla olajd cd,hla Èf.a /f.k hdu isÿ lrkafka fuu ia:rh u.ska fõ'
        tA i|yd IP ,smsk fhdod .kS'
    •   It is responsible for addressing, routing, and traffic control.
        ,smsklrKh" ud¾.lrKh" iy wjysr md,kh i|yd j.lsjhq;af;a fuh fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -56-                                          #ictfromabc



    •   Here, the IP addresses of the recipient and the sender are added to the received data as segments. Then

    DataLink layer / o;a; iïnkaOl ia:rh

        LKavhla yryd o;a; úYajdikSh f,i iïfm%aIKh lsÍu i|yd j.lSu orhs'

        rduq f,i meñKs o;a; ìgq njg mßj¾;kh ùu isÿ jkafka fuu ia:rh ;=,§ fõ'

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    User Datagram Protocol (UDP) is a protocol used to send data without establishing connection. It is known
    for its speed and simplicity, but does’nt guarantee delivery, ordering, or error checking like TCP does.
    User Datagram Protocol (UDP) hkq iïnkaO;djhla ia:dms; fkdlr o;a; heùug Ndú;d lrk

    lsÍu fyda fodaI mÍlaId lsÍu iy;sl fkdlrhs'

    Router / ud¾.lh
    A router is a key network device that manages and directs traffic between networks.
    ud¾.lhla cd, w;r .ukd.ukh l<ukdlrKh lrk iy fufyhjk m%Odk cd, Wmdx.hls'

    Inside a router, several essential components and devices work together to perform its functions.
    ud¾.lhla ;=<" tys ld¾hhka bgq lsÍu i|yd w;HjYH ixrpl iy Wmdx. lsysmhla tlg l%shd lrhs'

    A router is a computer and networking device that forwards data packets between computer networks,
    including internetworks such as the global Internet.
    ud¾.lh hkq f.da,Sh wka;¾cd,h jeks wka;¾cd, jev we;=¿j mß.Kl cd, w;r o;a; melÜ fhduq
    lrk mß.Kl iy cd,lrK Wmdx.hls'

    How Routers Work / ud¾.lhl l%shdldÍ;ajh
    When a data packet arrives at a router, the router examines the packet's header to identify its destination
    address.
    o;a; melÜgqjla rjqgrhlg meñKs úg" rjqgrh tys .ukdka; ,smskh y÷kd .ekSug melÜgqfõ
    YS¾Ih mÍlaId lrhs'

    Using a routing table, which contains information about available paths, the router determines the best
    next hop for the packet.
    mj;sk ud¾. ms<sn| f;dr;=re wvx.= ud¾..; lsÍfï j.=jla Ndú;d lrñka" rjqgrh melÜgqj i|yd
    fyd|u B<Õ fydma tl ;SrKh lrhs'

    This process is equal to a traveler consulting a map to find the best route to their destination.
    fuu l%shdj,sh ixpdrlfhl= ;u .ukdka;hg fyd|u ud¾.h fidhd .ekSug is;shula WmfoaYkh lsÍu
    yd iudk fõ'

    The router then forwards the packet to the next device or router along the path, continuing this process
    until the packet reaches its final destination.
    rjqgrh miqj melÜgqj B<Õ Wmdx.hg fyda ud¾.h Tiafia rjqgrhg fhduq lrhs" melÜgqj tys
    wjidk .ukdka;h lrd <Õd jk f;la fuu l%shdj,sh È.gu lrf.k hhs'

    Statement / m%ldYh A)

    Answer: 2)

    Cryptography / .=ma;fla;kh
    Cryptography is the process of hiding or coding information so that only the person a message was
    intended for can read it.
    .=ma;fla;kh hkq mKsúvhla woyia lrk mqoa.,hdg muKla th lshúh yels jk f,i f;dr;=re
    ieÕùu fyda fla;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs'

    Encryption is the process of converting plaintext (readable data) into ciphertext (encoded data) to protect
    it from unauthorized access.
    fla;kh hkq ir, fm< ^lshúh yels o;a;& wkjir m%fõYfhka wdrlaId lsÍu i|yd fla;s; fm<
    ^fla;.; o;a;& njg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs'

    Decryption is the reverse process, converting ciphertext back into plaintext so that authorized users can
    read it.
    úfla;kh hkq tys m%;sf,dau l%shdj,sh jk w;r" n,h,;a mßYS,lhskag lshúh yels mßÈ fla;s; fm<
    kej; idudkH fm< njg mßj¾;kh lrhs'

    Cryptographic Keys / .=ma;fla;k h;=re
    Keys are secret pieces of information used in encryption and decryption. The security of the encrypted
    data depends on keeping the key confidential.
    h;=re hkq fla;kh iy úfla;kh lsÍfï§ Ndú;d lrk ryia f;dr;=re fldgia fõ' fla;d;aul o;a;j,
    wdrlaIdj r|d mj;skafka h;=r ryis.;j ;nd .ekSu u;h'

    Encryption types

    1. Symmetric key encryption (private key encryption)
       iuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh ^mqoa.,sl h;=re fla;kh&

    2. Asymmetric key encryption (Public key encryption)
       wiuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh ^fmdÿ h;=re fla;kh&

    Symmetric key encryption / iuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh
    Symmetric key encryption, also known as private-key encryption, uses the same key for both encryption
    and decryption.
    mqoa.,sl h;=re fla;kh f,io ye¢kafjk iuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh" fla;kh iy úfla;kh hk
    folgu tlu h;=r Ndú;d lrhs'

    This means that the same key is used to encode the plaintext into ciphertext and to decode it back to
    plaintext.
    fuhska woyia lrkafka ir, fm, fla;kh lsÍug iy th kej; ir, fm<g úfla;kh lsÍug tlu
    h;=r Ndú;d lrk njhs'

    Asymmetric Key Encryption / wiuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh
    Asymmetric key encryption, also known as public-key encryption, uses a pair of keys
    fmdÿ h;=re fla;kh f,io ye¢kafjk wiuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh u.ska h;=re hq.,hla Ndú;d lrhs

    A public key and a private key.
    fmdÿ h;=rla iy mqoa.,sl h;=rla

    Each participant in a communication has their own unique pair of these keys.
    ikaksfõokhl isák iEu md¾Yjhlgu fuu h;=re hq.,hla we;'

    These keys are mathematically related, meaning that the encryption performed with one key can only be
    decrypted with the corresponding key.
    fuu h;=re .Ks;uh jYfhka iïnkaO fõ" tkï tla h;=rlska isÿ lrk fla;kh úfla;kh l<
    yelafla thg wod< wfkla h;=frka muKs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -60-                                          #ictfromabc


    If information is encrypted using the public key, only the corresponding private key can decrypt it and
    vice versa.
    fmdÿ h;=r Ndú;fhka f;dr;=re fla;kh lr we;akï" th úfla;kh l< yelafla thg wod< mqoa.,sl
    h;=rg muKs' th wfkla w;go isÿ l< yel'

    Public Key
    Can be shared openly and is used to encrypt data or verify signatures.
    újD;j fnod .; yels w;r o;a; fla;kh lsÍug fyda w;aika i;Hdmkh lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs'

    Private Key
    Must be kept secret and secure; it is used to decrypt data or create signatures.
    ryis.;j iy wdrlaIs;j ;nd .; hq;=h' th o;a; úfla;kh lsÍug fyda w;aika iE§ug Ndú;d lrhs

    Let's analyze each statement one by one. / wms tla tla m%ldYh tlska tl úYaf,aIKh lruq'

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    Incorrect / jerÈ
    In public key cryptography, priv(x) (private key) and pub(x) (public key) must be different.
    fmdÿ h;=re .=ma; fla;kfha§" priv(x) ^mqoa.,sl h;=r& iy pub(x) ^fmdÿ h;=r& fjkia úh hq;=h'

    Security relies on them being mathematically linked but not identical.
    wdrlaIdj r|d mj;skafka tAjd .Ks;uh jYfhka iïnkaO ù ;sîu u; kuq;a iudk fkdfõ'

    The public key is shared with everyone, and the private key is kept secret.
    fmdÿ h;=r iEu flfkl= iuÕu fnodf.k we;s w;r mqoa.,sl h;=r ryis.;j ;nd we;'

    If they were the same, the system would not be asymmetric, which is essential for public key cryptography.
    tAjd iudk kï" moaO;sh wiuñ;sl fkdjkq we;" th fmdÿ h;=re .=ma; fla;kh i|yd w;HjYH fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    Incorrect / jerÈ
    This describes symmetric encryption, not asymmetric.
    fuh wiuñ;sl fkdj iuñ;sl fla;kh úia;r lrhs'

    In public key systems / fmdÿ h;=re moaO;sj,
    A message encrypted with pub(x) is decrypted with priv(x).
    Pub(x) iuÕ fla;kh lrk ,o mKsúvhla priv(x) iuÕ úfla;kh lr we;'

    A message encrypted with priv(x) (e.g., for digital signatures) is decrypted with pub(x).
    Priv(x) iuÕ fla;kh lrk ,o mKsúvhla ^Wod( äðg,a w;aika i|yd& pub(x) iuÕ úfla;kh lr
    we;'

    tneúka" pub(x) yg th fla;kh l< mKsúvhla úfla;kh l< fkdyel'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    Correct / ksjerÈ
    The person x generates the key pair.
    x mqoa.,hd h;=re hq.,h ckkh lrhs'

    They keep priv(x) private and share pub(x) with others.

 11. Local Area Network (LAN) has 500 network devices. What is the most appropriate subnet mask for
     this computer network?
     ia:dkSh fmfoia cd,hl (LAN) cd, Wml%u (network devices) 500 la ;sfí' tu mß.Kl cd,h i|yd
     jvd;a u iqÿiq Wmcd, wdjrKh (subnet mask) l=ula o

    1) 255.255.255.0                   2) 255.255.255.128                 3) 255.255.255.192
    4) 255.255.255.224                 5) 255.255.254.0
                                                                                                       ( 2016 – 23 )
    Answer: 5)

    A LAN with 500 network devices requires at least 500 usable IP addresses. Let's analyze each subnet mask
    option:
    cd, Wmdx. 500la iys; LAN tllg wju jYfhka Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk 500la wjYH fõ' tla
    tla Wmcd, wdjrK úl,amh úYaf,aIKh lruq(

    Understanding Subnet Masks and Usable Ips / Wmcd, wdjrK iy Ndú;d l< yels IP f;areï .ekSu
    A subnet mask determines the network portion and the host portion of an IP address. The number of usable
    IP addresses in a subnet is calculated as 2h−2, where 'h' is the number of host bits (bits set to 0 in the subnet
    mask). We subtract 2 because the first address is the network address and the last is the broadcast address,
    neither of which can be assigned to a device.
    Wmcd, wdjrKhla IP ,smskhl cd, fldgi iy Odrl fldgi ;SrKh lrhs' Wmcd,hl Ndú;d l<
    yels IP ,smsk .Kk 2h−2 f,i .Kkh flf¾" tys§ h hkq Odrl ìgq .Kkhs ^Wmcd, wdjrKfha ìgq
    0 f,i ilid we;&' m<uq ,smskh cd, ,smskh jk w;r wjidk ,smskh úldYk ,smskh jk neúka wms
    2 wvq lruq" tA foflka tllaj;a Wmdx.hlg mejßh fkdyel'

    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    255.255.255.0
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111111.00000000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 8
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 8

    Usable IP addresses / Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 28 – 2 = 256 – 2 = 254.

    This is not enough for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500la i|yd m%udKj;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    255.255.255.128
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111111.10000000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 7
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 7

    Usable IP addresses / Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 27 −2 = 128 – 2 = 126.

    This is not enough for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500la i|yd m%udKj;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    255.255.255.192
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111111.11000000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 6
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 6
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                             -62-                                        #ictfromabc



    Usable IP addresses = 26 – 2 = 64 – 2 = 62.
    Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 26 – 2 = 64 – 2 = 62.

    This is not enough for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500la i|yd m%udKj;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    255.255.255.224
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111111.11100000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 5
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 5

    Usable IP addresses = 25 – 2 = 32 – 2 = 30.
    Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 25 – 2 = 32 – 2 = 30.

    This is not enough for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500la i|yd m%udKj;a fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    255.255.254.0
    In binary, this is 11111111.11111111.11111110.00000000.

    Number of host bits (h) = 9 (1 bit from the third octet + 8 bits from the fourth octet)
    Odrl ìgq .Kk (h) = 9 ^;=kajk wIaGufhka ìgq 1la + y;rjk wIaGufhka ìgq 8la&

    Usable IP addresses = 29 – 2 = 512 – 2 = 510.
    Ndú;d l< yels IP ,smsk = 29 – 2 = 512 – 2 = 510.

    This is sufficient for 500 devices.
    fuh Wmdx. 500 lg m%udKj;a fõ'

    Therefore, option 5) is the correct answer.

   1. In electronic mail systems, protocol used by mail clients to retrieve messages from the mail server is

          1) Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP).                                                2) File Transfer Protocol (FTP).

          Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP)

          SMTP does not retrieve or read emails - that’s handled by IMAP or POP3.

          fiajdodhlfhka Yahoo ys fiajdodhlhg udre lrhs'

          In summary, SMTP is used to send emails from one computer to another over a network.

          Ndú;d lrhs'

    IMAP is the protocol used by mail clients to retrieve emails from the mail server. It allows users to view

 2. The transport layer protocol User Datagram Protocol (UDP) can be used for
    User Datagram Protocol (UDP) kï m%jdyk ia:r kshudj,sh ................ i|yd Ndú; l< yelsh'
    by; j.ka;sfha ysia;ek msrùu i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq jkafka my; i|yka ljrla o?

    1) reliable communication / úYajikSh ikaksfõokh
    2) guaranteed delivery / iy;sl lrk ,o fnod yeÍu
    3) connection oriented communication / iïnkaOkdNsuqL ikaksfõokh
    4) ordered delivery / l%udkql+, fnod yeÍu
    5) exchanging state information among routers / uxyiqre w;r ;;a;aj f;dr;=re yqjudre lsÍu

                                                                                             ( 2017 - 17 )
    Answer: 5)
    User Datagram Protocol (UDP) is one of the core protocols of the Internet Protocol (IP) suite. It is a
    connectionless and lightweight transport layer protocol used to send data packets between computers on
    a network. / User Datagram Protocol (UDP) hkq wka;¾cd, fm%dfgdaflda,(IP) lÜg,fha uQ,sl fm%dfgdaflda,
    j,ska tlls' th cd,hl mß.Kl w;r o;a; melÜ heùug Ndú;d lrk iïnkaO;d rys; iy ieye,a¨
    m%jdyk ia:r fm%dfgdaflda,hls'

    Key Features of UDP / UDP ys m%Odk úfYaIdx.

    Connectionless / iïnkaO;djh rys;

    •   No connection is established before data is sent.
        o;a; heùug fmr iïnkaO;djhla ia:dms; fkdflf¾'

    •   Each packet (called a datagram) is sent independently.
        iEu melÜgqjlau ^datagram f,i ye¢kafõ& iajdëkj hjkq ,efí'

    Unreliable Delivery / úYajdi l< fkdyels fnodyeÍu

    •   UDP does not guarantee delivery, order, or protection against duplication.
        UDP wkqmsgm;a lsÍug tfrysj fnod yeÍu" weKjqu fyda wdrlaIdj iy;sl fkdlrhs'

    •   There is no error correction or acknowledgment like in TCP.
        TCP ys fuka fodaI ksjerÈ lsÍula fyda ms<s.ekSula fkdue;'

    Faster Transmission / fõ.j;a iïfm%aIKh

    •   Less overhead makes UDP faster than TCP.
        wvq Wäia n,h UDP TCP j,g jvd fõ.j;a lrhs'

    •   Suitable for time-sensitive applications.
        ld, ixfõ§ fhÿï i|yd iqÿiq fõ'

    No Flow or Congestion Control / m%jdy fyda ;onoh md,khla fkdue;
    UDP does not manage how much data is sent or how fast.
    UDP fldmuK o;a; hjkjdo fyda fldmuK fõ.j;ao hkak l<ukdlrKh fkdlrhs'

    Port Numbers
    Uses port numbers to distinguish between different services or applications.

 4. The first and the last IP addresses of a subnet are 192.192.48.0 and 192.192.63.255 respectively.
    Which of the followings is the subnet mask of this subnet?
    Wmcd,hl m<uq iy wjidk IP ,smsk ms<sfj<ska 192.192.48.0 iy 192.192.63.255 fõ' fuu Wmcd,fha
    Wmcd, wdjrKh jkafka my; oelafjk oE w;=frka l=ula o?

    1) 255.255.255.0                           2) 255.255.192.0                   3) 255.255.255.192
    4) 255.255.240.0                           5) 255.240.0.0
                                                                                                      ( 2017 - 19 )
    Answer: 4)

    1   Analyze the changing octets. / fjkia jk wIaGu úYaf,aIKh lrkak'
        First IP: 192.192.48.0
        Last IP: 192.192.63.255
        The first two octets (192.192) are the same, meaning they are part of the network portion of the address
        and the subnet mask will have 255 in those positions. / m<uq wIaGu fol (192.192) iudk fõ" tkï
        tAjd ,smskfha cd, fldgfiys fldgila jk w;r Wmcd, wdjrKfha tu ia:dkj, 255 la we;'
        The third octet changes from 48 to 63. The fourth octet changes from 0 to 255. This indicates that the
        subnetting occurs within third octet. / ;=kajk wIaglh 48 isg 63 olajd fjkia fõ' isõjk wIaglh
        0 isg 255 olajd fjkia fõ' fuhska weÕfjkafka ;=kajk wIaglh ;=< Wmcd,lrKh isÿjk njhs'

    2   Convert the changing octets to binary. / fjkia jk wIaGuh oaúuh njg mßj¾;kh lrkak'
        Let's look at the third octet.
        ;=kajk wIaGuh foi n,uq'
        48 in binary: 00110000
        63 in binary: 00111111

    3   Identify the network and host bits. / cd,h iy Odrl ìgq y÷kd .kak'
        Look at the binary representations of 48 and 63. The bits that are common from left to right represent
        the network portion. The bits that change represent the host portion.
        48 iy 63 ys oaúuh ksrEmKhka foi n,kak' jfï isg ol=Kg fmdÿ ìgq cd, fldgi ksfhdackh
        lrhs' fjkia jk ìgq Odrl fldgi ksfhdackh lrhs'
        00110000 (48)
        00111111 (63)
        The first four bits (0011) are common. This means these are network bits. The remaining four bits
        (xxxx) in the third octet are host bits. / m<uq ìgq y;r (0011) fmdÿ fõ' fuhska woyia lrkafka fïjd
        cd, ìgq njhs' ;=kajk wIaGufha b;sß ìgq y;r (xxxx) Odrl ìgq fõ'

    4   Construct the subnet mask. / Wmcd, wdjrKh idokak'
        For the first two octets, the mask is 255.255 because they don't change.
        m<uq wIagl fol i|yd" tAjd fjkia fkdjk ksid wdjrKh 255.255 fõ'
        For the third octet: The first four bits are network bits (1s in the subnet mask) and the last four are host
        bits (0s in the subnet mask). / ;=kajk wIaglh i|yd( m<uq ìgq y;r cd, ìgq ^Wmcd, wdjrKfha
        1& jk w;r wjidk y;r Odrl ìgq ^Wmcd, wdjrKfha 0& fõ'
        11110000 in binary = 240 in decimal.
        For the fourth octet: Since the host range goes from 0 to 255, all 8 bits are host bits, meaning 0 in the
        subnet mask. / y;rjk wIaGuh i|yd( Odrl mrdih 0 isg 255 olajd hk neúka" ish¨u ìgq 8
        Odrl ìgq fõ" tkï Wmcd, wdjrKfha 0 fõ'

    Therefore, the subnet mask is 255.255.240.0
    tneúka" Wmcd, wdjrKh 255.255.240.0 fõ
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -71-                                         #ictfromabc


 5. 172.16.48.200/24 is a
    172.16.48.200/24 hk
