    Key Points of MCQ

    (i) Client Server / fiajd,dNS fiajdodhl
        The Client-Server model is a fundamental architecture in network computing where multiple clients
        (users or devices) request and receive services from a centralized server.
        fiajd,dNS fiajdodhl wdlD;sh hkq nyq fiajdodhlhka ^mßYS,lhska fyda Wmdx.& uOH.;
        fiajdodhlhlska fiajd b,a,d isák iy ,nd .kakd cd, mß.Kkfha uQ,sl wdlD;s Ys,amhls'

       Client / fiajdodhlhd
       A client is any device or software that requests a service or resource from a server. Clients are
       typically end-user devices such as computers, smartphones, or tablets, running applications like web
       browsers, email clients, or point-of-sale systems.
       fiajdodhlfhla hkq fiajdodhlhlska fiajdjla fyda iïm;la b,a,d isák ´kEu Wmdx.hla fyda
       uDÿldx.hls' fiajd,dNSka idudkHfhka mß.Kl" iaud¾Ü f*daka fyda geí,Ü jeks wjidk mßYS,l
       Wmdx." fjí n%jqir" Bfï,a fiajd,dNSka" fyda úl=Kqï ia:dk moaO;s jeks fhÿï Odjkh lrhs'

       Server / fiajdodhlh
       A server is a powerful computer or software application that provides resources, data, or services to
       clients over a network. Servers can host websites, manage databases, provide email services, and more.
       fiajdodhlhla hkq cd,hla yryd fiajdodhlhskag iïm;a" o;a; fyda fiajd imhk n,j;a
       mß.Klhla fyda uDÿldx. fhÿuls' fiajdodhlhkag fjí wvú i;aldrl;ajh" o;a; iuqod
       l<ukdlrKh" Bfï,a fiajd iemhSu iy ;j;a fndfyda foa l< yel'

       Key Characteristics of the Client-Server Model
       fiajd,dNS fiajdodhl wdlD;sfha m%Odk ,laIK

       (i) Centralization / uOH.; lsÍu
           In a client-server model, the server is centralized, meaning it handles multiple client requests,
           manages data, and provides services from a single location. This centralization allows for easier
           management, security, and updates.
           fiajd,dNS-fiajdodhl wdlD;shla ;=<" fiajdodhlh uOH.; fõ" tkï th nyq fiajdodhl b,a,Sï
           yiqrejhs" o;a; l<ukdlrKh lrhs" iy tla ia:dkhl isg fiajd imhhs' fuu uOH.;lrKh
           myiq l<ukdlrKh" wdrlaIdj iy hdj;ald,Sk lsÍï i|yd bv i,ihs'

       (ii) Scalability / mßudKh
            Servers can be scaled up to handle many clients simultaneously, either by upgrading the server
            hardware or by distributing the load across multiple servers (load balancing).
            fiajdodhl oDVdx. W;afY%aKs lsÍu u.ska fyda nyq fiajdodhlhka ^Ndr ;=,kh lsÍu& yryd
            Ndrh fnod yeÍu u.ska" fndfyda fiajd,dNSka tljr yeisrùug fiajdodhlhka mßudKh l<
            yel'

       (iii) Resource Sharing / iïm;a fnod.ekSu
           Clients do not need to store all resources locally. Instead, they can access resources like files,
           databases, or applications stored on the server. fiajdodhlhskag ish¨u iïm;a foaYShj .nvd
           lsÍug wjYH fkdfõ' tA fjkqjg" Tjqkag fiajdodhlfha .nvd lr we;s f.dkq" o;a; iuqodhka
           fyda fhÿï jeks iïm;a fj; m%fõY úh yel'

       (iv) Security / wdrlaIdj

           authorized clients can access certain services or data.

    Plaintext / ir, fm<
    Plaintext refers to the original, readable form of data or information before it has been encrypted. This is
    the data that is understandable and can be directly read and interpreted by humans or computers without
    needing any decryption. In the context of cryptography and data security, plaintext is the starting point for
    encryption and the end result of decryption.
    ir, fm< hkq .=ma;fla;kh lsÍug fmr o;a; fyda f;dr;=re j, uq,a" lshúh yels wdldrhhs' fuh
    f;areï .; yels o;a; jk w;r lsisÿ úfla;khla wjYH fkdù ñksiqkag fyda mß.Klj,g fl,skau
    lshjd w¾:l:kh l< yelsh' .=ma;fla;kh iy o;a; wdrlaIK ikao¾Nh ;=<" ir, fm< hkq
    fla;kh i|yd wdrïNl ,laIHh iy úfla;kfha wjidk m%;sM,hhs'

    Ciphertext / fla;sl fm<
    Ciphertext is the result of encryption performed on plaintext using an encryption algorithm and a key. This
    transformed data is not readable or understandable without the appropriate decryption key and process.
    Ciphertext ensures that the information is secure and protected from unauthorized access during storage
    or transmission. / fla;sl fm< hkq fla;dxlk we,af.dß;uhla iy h;=rla Ndú;fhka plaintext u; isÿ
    lrk ,o fla;khl m%;sM,hls' fhda.H úfla;k h;=r iy l%shdj,sh fkdue;sj fuu mßj¾;kh lrk
    ,o o;a; lshúh yels fyda f;areï .; fkdyel' .nvd lsÍfï§ fyda iïfm%aIKh lsÍfï§ f;dr;=re
    iqrlaIs; nj iy wkjirfhka m%fõY ùfuka wdrlaId ùu Ciphertext iy;sl lrhs'

    Encryption / .=ma;fla;kh
    Encryption is the process of converting plaintext (readable data) into ciphertext (an unreadable format) to
    protect it from unauthorized access. This process ensures that only authorized parties can read the data by
    transforming it back into its original form through decryption. / .=ma;fla;kh hkq wkjir m%fõYhlska
    wdrlaId lsÍu i|yd ir, fm< ^lshúh yels o;a;& fla;dxl fm< ^lshúh fkdyels wdlD;shla& njg
    mßj¾;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs' fuu l%shdj,sh úfla;kh yryd o;a; kej; tys uq,a iajrEmhg

    2) Asymmetric Key Encryption / wiuñ;sl h;=re .=ma;fla;kh

    Decryption / úfla;kh
    Decryption is the process of converting ciphertext (encrypted data) back into its original form, known as
    plaintext. This process allows authorized users to access and read the original data. Decryption uses a
    specific algorithm and a key to transform the ciphertext back into readable data.
    úfla;kh hkq fla;dxl fm< ^.=ma;fla;kh lrk ,o o;a;& kej; tys uq,a iajrEmhg mßj¾;kh

    There are 2 ways. / wdldr 2ls'

    1) Symmetric Key Decryption / iuñ;sl h;=re úfla;kh
        Uses the same key for both encryption and decryption.

    To determine the correct answer, let's analyze each statement in the context of the message conversion
    from HELLO to IFMMP.
    ksjerÈ ms<s;=r ksYaph lsÍu i|yd" HELLO isg IFMMP fj; mKsúv mßj¾;kfha ikao¾Nh ;=<
    tla tla m%ldYh úYaf,aIKh lruq'

    A. HELLO is the plaintext while IFMMP is the ciphertext.
       HELLO ir, fm< (plaintext) jk w;r IFMMP fla;sl fm< (ciphertext) fõ'
        Correct, The original message HELLO is known as the plaintext, and after encryption, it is converted
        to IFMMP, which is the ciphertext. This is a standard way of describing encrypted messages.
        ksjerÈhs" uq,a mKsúvh HELLO ir, fm< f,i y÷kajkq ,nk w;r" ixfla;dxlkh lsÍfuka
        miqj" th fla;dxl mdGh jk IFMMP njg mßj¾;kh fõ' fuh ixfla;d;aul mKsúv úia;r
        lsÍfï iïu; l%uhls'

    B. IFMMP is the result of applying the ASCII code to HELLO.
       IFMMP hkq HELLO u; ASCII fla;h fhojQ úg ,efnk m%;sM,hhs'
        Incorrect, The conversion from HELLO to IFMMP is not a direct application of ASCII encoding.
        Instead, it appears to be a simple Caesar cipher where each letter in HELLO is shifted by +1 in the
        alphabet to produce IFMMP. ASCII involves numerical representations of characters, not the kind of
        character shift seen here.
        jerÈhs" HELLO isg IFMMP fj; mßj¾;kh ASCII fla;kfha iDcq fhÿula fkdfõ' tA fjkqjg"
        th ir, iSi¾ fla;dxlhla f,i fmfka" tys§ HELLO ys iEu wl=rlau IFMMP ksmoùug fydaäfha
        +1 u.ska udre lrkq ,efí' ASCII fuys olakg ,efnk wdldrfha wlaIr udrejla fkdj" wlaIr
        ixLHd;aul ksrEmKhka we;=<;a fõ'

    C. +1 is the encryption key while -1 is the decryption key.
       fla;k h;=r (encryption key) +1 pk w;r Ñfla;k h;=r (decryption key) -1 fõ'
        Correct, The transformation from HELLO to IFMMP involves shifting each letter by +1 in the
        alphabet. For example, 'H' becomes 'I', 'E' becomes 'F', etc. The key used for this encryption is +1. To
        decrypt IFMMP back to HELLO, you would apply a -1 shift, making -1 the decryption key.
        ksjerÈhs" HELLO isg IFMMP olajd mßj¾;khg tla tla wl=r fydaäfha +1 u.ska udre lsÍu
        we;=<;a fõ' WodyrKhla f,i" H I njg m;a fõ" E F njg m;a fõ" wdÈh' fuu ixfla;kh i|yd
        Ndú;d lrk h;=r +1 fõ' IFMMP kej; HELLO fj; úfla;kh lsÍug" Tn -1 úfla;k h;=r
        lrñka -1 udrejla fhdokq we;'

    Summary / idrdxYh

    A is correct: HELLO is the plaintext, and IFMMP is the ciphertext.
    A ksjerÈhs( HELLO hkq ir, mdGh jk w;r IFMMP hkq fla;dxl mdGhhs'

    B is incorrect: The conversion is not related to ASCII coding but rather a Caesar cipher shift.
    B jerÈhs( mßj¾;kh ASCII fla;khg iïnkaO fkdj iSi¾ fla;dxl udrejls'

    C is correct: The encryption key is +1, and the decryption key is -1.
    C ksjerÈhs( ixfla;dxlk h;=r +1 jk w;r úfla;k h;=r -1 fõ'

    When there are multiple computers in an office, each computer can be given a private IP address. The

    Which of the following is the correct combination for the blanks A, B and C?

    1)   A - private, B - file server, C - HTTP
    2)   A - private, B - Internet, C - DHCP
    3)   A - private, B - router, C - FTP
    4)   A - public, B - file server, C - FTP

    Private IP Address / mqoa.,sl IP ,smskh
    A private IP address is an IP address that is used within a private network, such as a home, office, or
    enterprise network. These addresses are not routable on the public internet, meaning they are reserved for
    use within local networks and cannot be accessed directly from the outside world.
    mqoa.,sl IP ,smskhla hkq ksjila" ld¾hd,hla fyda jHjidh cd,hla jeks mqoa.,sl cd,hla ;=< Ndú;d
    jk IP ,smskhls' fuu ,smsk fmdÿ wka;¾cd,fha .uka l< fkdyel" tkï tAjd foaYSh cd, ;=< Ndú;h
    i|yd fjka lr we;s w;r ndysr f,dalfhka iDcqj m%fõY úh fkdyel'

    Range of Private IP Addresses
    mqoa.,sl IP ,smsk mrdih

    Class A: 10.0.0.0 to 10.255.255.255 (10.0.0.0/8)
    Class B: 172.16.0.0 to 172.31.255.255 (172.16.0.0/12)
    Class C: 192.168.0.0 to 192.168.255.255 (192.168.0.0/16)

    These ranges are designated for private use and are not assigned to any specific organization. They can be
    freely used within any local network.
    fuu mrdihka mqoa.,sl Ndú;h i|yd kï lr we;s w;r lsisÿ ksYaÑ; ixúOdkhlg mjrd fkdue;'
    ´kEu foaYSh cd,hla ;=< tAjd ksoyfia Ndú;d l< yelsh'

    Local Network Use / foaYSh cd, Ndú;h
    Private IP addresses are used to identify devices within a local network (e.g., a home or office LAN). Each
    device on the network is assigned a unique private IP address by router,usually through the DHCP protocol.
    mqoa.,sl IP ,smsk foaYSh cd,hla ;=< Wmdx. y÷kd .ekSug Ndú;d lrhs ^Wod( ksji fyda ld¾hd, LAN&
    cd,fha iEu Wmdx.hlgu idudkHfhka DHCP fm%dfgdaflda,h yryd rjqgrh u.ska wkkH mqoa.,sl IP
    ,smskhla mjrkq ,efí'

    Public IP Address / fmdÿ IP ,smskh
    A public IP address is an IP address that is assigned to a device that is directly accessible over the internet.
    Unlike private IP addresses, which are used within local networks and are not visible to the outside world,
    public IP addresses are unique across the entire internet and are used to identify devices such as servers,
    routers, and other networking equipment that need to communicate with other devices on global internet.
    fmdÿ IP ,smskhla hkq wka;¾cd,h yryd iDcqju m%fõY úh yels Wmdx.hlg mjrd we;s IP ,smskhls'

    Guided media / kshuq udOH
    Guided media refers to the transmission of data over a physical path or medium. This type of media
    provides a clear, defined route for the signal to travel from the sender to the receiver. Guided media is
    essential in wired communication systems, where the physical connection ensures that the data is
    transmitted efficiently and with minimal interference.
    kshuq udOH hkq fN!;sl ud¾.hla fyda udOHhla yryd o;a; iïfm%aIKh lsÍuhs' fuu j¾.fha udOH
    uÕska ix{dj hjkakdf.a isg .%dylhd olajd .uka lsÍu i|yd meyeÈ,s" ksYaÑ; ud¾.hla imhhs'
    /yeka.; ikaksfõok moaO;sj, ud¾f.damfoaYl udOH w;HjYH jk w;r" fN!;sl iïnkaO;djh o;a;
    ld¾hlaIuj iy wju ueÈy;aùï iys;j iïfm%aIKh jk nj iy;sl lrhs'

    Non Guided Media / kshuq fkdjk udOH
    Non-guided media, also known as unguided media or wireless media, refers to the method of transmitting
    data without using a physical conductor. Instead of traveling through cables or optical fibers, signals in
    non-guided media are broadcast through the air, vacuum, or even water. This type of transmission relies
    on electromagnetic waves, such as radio waves, microwaves, or infrared signals, to carry data from one
    point to another.
    kshuq fkdjk udOH fyda /yeka rys; udOH f,io ye¢kafjk ud¾..; fkdjk udOH " fN!;sl

    Router / ud¾.ldrlh
    A router is a critical networking device that connects multiple networks and directs data traffic between
    them. It serves as an intermediary, managing the flow of data packets between devices within a local
    network (such as computers, smartphones, and printers) and external networks (like the internet). Whether
    at home or in a large enterprise, routers enable efficient, secure, and reliable communication, ensuring that
    data gets where it needs to go.

     What is a MAC Address?

     interface controller (NIC) for use as a network address in communications within a network segment.

  4. An organization with the assigned IP address block 193.1.1.0/24 needs to define eight subnets. Each
     subnet should provide for more than 25 IP addresses. Which of the following correctly lists the
     number of bits needed to identify the given network, the total number of bits needed to identify the
     subnets, and the number of bits needed to assign unique IP addresses for this requirement,
     respectively? IP ,smsk 193.1.1.0/24 ldKavh mjrd we;s ixúOdkhlg Wmcd, wgla iE§ug wjYH

     1) 24, 3, 5           2) 24, 5, 3             3) 24, 27, 5         4) 27, 3, 5         5) 27, 30, 2

     Answer : 3 )
     Let's build the answer step by step.
     mshjfrka mshjr ms<s;=r f.dv k.uq'

     Thus, it appears that there are 24 bits for the network part and 8 bits for the host part (because an IPv4
     address consists of 32 bits in total).

     ^IPv4 ,smskhla uq¿ ìgq 32 lska iukaú; ksid&'

     i;aldrl i|yd b;sßj we;s ìgq .Kk ;SrKh lruq'
     From the original 8 bits for hosts, we use 3 bits for subnetting. This leaves us with 5 bits for hosts.

     5la b;sß lrhs'

     i;aldrl fldgi i|yd ìgq 5la iuÕska" Wmcd,hlg ;sfnk IP ,smsk .Kk 25 = 32 fõ'
     However, we need to subtract 2 addresses (network address and broadcast address) which leaves us with
     30 usable IP addresses per subnet.
     flfia fj;;a" wmg ,smsk 2la ^cd, ,smskh iy úldYk ,smskh& wvq lsÍug isÿ jk w;r tuÕska
     Wmcd,hlg Ndú; l< yels IP ,smsk 30la wmg b;sß fõ'
     Since 30 usable addresses satisfy the requirement of more than 25 IP addresses per subnet, this works.
     Ndú; l< yels ,smsk 30 ka tla Wmcd,hlg IP ,smsk 25lg jvd wjYH jk neúka" fuh l%shdlrhs'
     Accordingly, the correct bit allocation is,
     tA wkqj ksjerÈ ìgq fjkalsÍu jkafka"
     The number of bits required to identify the network is - 24 bits
     cd,h yÿkd.ekSug wjYH ìgq .Kk jkafka - ìgq 24

     The total number of bits required to identify the subnet is - Number of network bits + Number of subnet
     bits = 27 bits

     Network topology refers to the way different elements in a network are arranged, including devices,
     connections, and data flow. It defines how data travels from one device to another on the network.

     Bus Topology nia ia:,lh
     Bus topology is a network layout where all devices are connected to a single main cable, often called the
     backbone. In a network, data travels through this cable in both directions, and every device on the network
     receives the same signal simultaneously.

     Mesh topology oe,a ia:,lh
     Mesh topology is a web-like network structure where
     devices are interconnected with each other, as opposed to

     The Seven Layers of the OSI Model
     OSI udÈ,sfha ia;r y;

     1. Physical Layer (Layer 1)
        fN!;sl ia;rh ^m<uq ia;rh&

         and reception of raw binary data over a physical medium such as cables, radio waves, or fiber optics.

         using MAC addresses.

     4. Transport Layer (Layer 4)

     Digital Signature wxls; w;aik

     A digital signature is a cryptographic technique used to ensure the authenticity, integrity, and non-
     repudiation of a digital message or document. It functions similarly to a handwritten signature or a stamped
     seal, but it provides far more inherent security. Digital signatures are commonly used in various digital
     transactions, communications, and data exchanges to verify the identity of the sender and the integrity of
     the message.
     wxls; w;aikla hkq äðg,a mKsúvhl fyda f,aLkhl i;H;dj" wLKav;dj iy m%;slafIam fkdlsÍu
     iy;sl lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk .=ma; f,aLk Ys,amSh l%uhls' th w;ska ,shk ,o w;aiklg fyda uqød
     ;enQ uqødjlg iudkj l%shd lrhs" kuq;a th jvd;a wdfõksl wdrlaIdjla imhhs' hjkakdf.a wkkH;djh
     iy mKsúvfha wLKav;dj ;yjqre lsÍu i|yd úúO äðg,a .kqfokq" ikaksfõokh iy o;a; yqjudre
     j,§ äðg,a w;aika nyq,j Ndú;d fõ'

     Asymmetric Key Encryption wiuñ;sl .=ma; fla;kh
     Asymmetric key encryption, also known as public-key cryptography, is a type of encryption that uses a
     pair of keys for secure communication: a public key and a private key. These keys are mathematically
     related but distinct. The public key is used for encryption, while the private key is used for decryption.
     This system addresses the key distribution problem of symmetric key encryption, where the same key is
     used for both encryption and decryption.
     wiuñ;sl .=ma; fla;kh" fmdÿ h;=re .=ma;fla;kh f,io ye¢kafõ" th wdrlaIs; ikaksfõokh i|yd
     h;=re hq.,hla Ndú;d lrk ixfla;dxlk j¾.hls' tkï fmdÿ h;=rla iy mqoa.,sl h;=rla fú' fuu

     mqoa.,sl h;=r úfla;kh i|yd Ndú;d fõ' fuu moaO;sh fla;kh iy úfla;kh hk folgu tlu
     h;=r Ndú;d lrk iuñ;sl h;=re ixfla;dxlkfha m%Odk fnodyeÍfï .eg¨j úiohs'

     Can be freely distributed and shared with anyone.

     Encryption .=ma;fla;kh
     Encryption is the process of converting plaintext (readable data) into ciphertext (an unreadable format) to
     protect it from unauthorized access. This process ensures that only authorized parties can read the data by
     transforming it back into its original form through decryption.
     .=ma;fla;kh hkq wkjir m%fõYhlska wdrlaId lsÍu i|yd ir, fm< ^lshúh yels o;a;& fla;dxl
     fm< ^lshúh fkdyels wdlD;shla& njg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs' fuu l%shdj,sh úfla;kh yryd

     iy;sl lrhs'

     1) Symmetric Key Encryption

     2) Asymmetric Key Encryption

         h;=rla fõ'

     Decryption úfla;kh

     Decryption is the process of converting ciphertext (encrypted data) back into its original form, known as
     plaintext. This process allows authorized users to access and read the original data. Decryption uses a
     specific algorithm and a key to transform the ciphertext back into readable data.
     úfla;kh hkq fla;dxl fm< ^.=ma;fla;kh lrk ,o o;a;& kej; tys uq,a iajrEmhg mßj¾;kh

     Plaintext ir, fm<
     Plaintext refers to the original, readable form of data or information before it has been encrypted. This is
     the data that is understandable and can be directly read and interpreted by humans or computers without
     needing any decryption. In the context of cryptography and data security, plaintext is the starting point for
     encryption and the end result of decryption.
     ir, fm< hkq .=ma;fla;kh lsÍug fmr o;a; fyda f;dr;=re j, uq,a" lshúh yels wdldrhhs' fuh
     f;areï .; yels o;a; jk w;r lsisÿ úfla;khla wjYH fkdù ñksiqkag fyda mß.Klj,g fl,skau
     lshjd w¾:l:kh l< yelsh' .=ma;fla;kh iy o;a; wdrlaIK ikao¾Nh ;=<" ir, fm< hkq
     fla;kh i|yd wdrïNl ,laIHh iy úfla;kfha wjidk m%;sM,hhs'

     Ciphertext fla;sl fm<
     Ciphertext is the result of encryption performed on plaintext using an encryption algorithm and a key. This
     transformed data is not readable or understandable without the appropriate decryption key and process.
     Ciphertext ensures that the information is secure and protected from unauthorized access during storage
     or transmission.
     fla;sl fm< hkq fla;dxlk we,af.dß;uhla iy h;=rla Ndú;fhka ir, fm< u; isÿ lrk ,o

     Data communication protocols are sets of rules and conventions that govern the exchange of data between
     devices over a network. These protocols ensure that data is transmitted accurately, efficiently, and securely.
     Here are some key data communication protocols categorized by the OSI model layers they operate in:
     o;a; ikaksfõok kshudj,s hkq cd,hla yryd Wmdx. w;r o;a; yqjudrej md,kh lrk Í;s iy

            Ex : Port: 53.

                specific traffic based on predefined security rules. It acts as a packet filter by inspecting packets
                entering and leaving the network.

                A Trojan Horse is a type of malware that disguises itself as a legitimate or benign software program
                to trick users into installing it. Once installed, it can perform malicious activities.

 4. Select the answer containing the correct replacements for A and B in the following paragraph:
    my; fþofha A iy B ysia;ekaj,g iqÿiq wdfoaYl iys; ms<s;=r f;dard.kak'

    In the Internet, a host is identified by its IP address. In IPv4, each IP address consists of ………A………
    bits to identify a host. The newer version named IPv6 consists of ………B……… bits in an IP address.
    wka;¾cd,fha§" i;aldrlhla (host) tys IP fhduqfjka (address) y÷kd.efka' IPv4 ys§ i;aldrl
    y÷kd.ekSug iEu IP fhduqjlau ìgq ………A……… lska iukaú; fõ' IPv6 f,i y÷kajk kj ixialrKfha§
    IP fhduqjla ìgq ………B……… lska iukaú; fõ'

    1) A=32, B=48        2) A=32, B=128        3) A=48, B=32        4) A=48, B=128        5) A=128, B=32

                                                                                                 ( 2023 - 20 )
    Answer : 2 )
    Explanation
    meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    In IPv4, an IP address consists of 32 bits. These bits are usually represented as four octets separated by
    dots (Example: - 192.168.1.1).
    IPv4 ys" IP ,smskhla ìgq 32 lska iukaú; fõ' fuu ìgq idudkHfhka ;s;a j,ska fjka lrk ,o wIaguh
    y;rla f,i ksrEmKh flf¾ ^WodyrK( 192.168.1.1&'

    In IPv6, an IP address consists of 128 bits. These addresses are represented as eight groups of four
    hexadecimal digits separated by colons (Example: - 2001:0db8:85a3:0000:0000:8a2e:0370:7334).
    IPv6 ys" IP ,smskhla ìgq 128 lska iukaú; fõ' fuu ,smsk ;Sre j,ska fjka lrk ,o Iâ oYuh
    ixLHd y;rl ldKav wgla f,i ksrEmKh flf¾

    ^WodyrK 2001:0db8:85a3:0000:0000:8a2e:0370:7334&'

    So, the correct replacements are A = 32 and B = 128, which corresponds to Answer 2.
    tneúka" ksjerÈ wdfoaYkh A = 32 iy B = 128 ms<s;=r 2 g wkqrEm fõ'

    Domain Name System - jiï kdu moaO;sh (DNS)
    The Domain Name System (DNS) is an essential part of the internet's infrastructure. It is responsible for
    translating human-readable domain names, such as www.ictfromabc.com, into machine-readable IP
    addresses, such as 192.0.2.1.
    jiï kdu moaO;sh (DNS)            hkq wka;¾cd,fha há;, jHqyfha w;HjYH fldgils' th
    www.ictfromabc.com jeks ñksiqkag lshúh yels jiï kdu" 192.0.2.1 jeks hka;% lshúh yels IP ,smsk
    njg mßj¾;kh lsÍu isÿ lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -163-                                          #ictfromabc


    DNS translates domain names to IP addresses, which are used by computers to locate and communicate
    with each other on the internet. This allows users to use easy-to-remember domain names instead of having
    to remember numerical IP addresses.
    DNS jiï kdu IP ,smsk j,g mßj¾;kh lrhs" tAjd wka;¾cd,fha ia:dk.; lsÍug iy tlsfkld
    iuÕ ikaksfõokh lsÍug mß.Kl Ndú;d lrhs' fuh mßYS,lhskag ixLHd;aul IP ,smsk u;l ;nd

    Hypertext Transfer Protocol - f,dal jHdma; úisß úhuk (HTTP)
    HTTP is a protocol used for transmitting hypertext (HTML) documents and other data on the World Wide
    Web. It serves as the foundation of data communication on the web, enabling the retrieval of web pages,
    images, videos, and other resources.

 7. If Suresh wants to send an encrypted message to be read only by Amara using asymmetric key
    encryption, then
    wiuñ;sl h;=re fla;kh fhdod .=ma; fla;kh (encrypt) l< mKsúvhla wurg muKla lshùu i|yd
    heùug iqf¾Iag wjYH kï"
    1) Suresh should encrypt his message using his public key.
       iqf¾Ia ;u fmdÿ h;=r fhdod mKsjqvh .=ma; fla;kh l< hq;=h'
    2) Suresh should encrypt his message using his private key.
       iqf¾Ia ;u fm!oa.,sl h;=r fhdod mKsjqvh .=ma; fla;kh l< hq;=h'
    3) Suresh should encrypt his message using Amara’s public key.
       iqf¾Ia" wurf.a fmdÿ h;=r fhdod mKsjqvh .=ma; fla;kh l< hq;=h'
    4) Suresh should encrypt his message using Amara’s private key.
       iqf¾Ia" wurf.a fm!oa.,sl h;=r fhdod mKsjqvh .=ma; fla;kh l< hq;=h'
    5) Suresh should encrypt his message using both private and public keys of Amara.
       iqf¾Ia" wurf.a fmdÿ iy fm!oa.,sl hk h;=re folu fhdod mKsjqvh .=ma; fla;kh l< hq;=h'

                                                                                                 ( 2023 - 23 )
    Answer : 3 )
    Asymmetric Key Encryption - wiuñ;sl .=ma; fla;kh
    Asymmetric key encryption, also known as public-key cryptography, is a type of encryption that uses a
    pair of keys for secure communication: a public key and a private key. These keys are mathematically
    related but distinct. The public key is used for encryption, while the private key is used for decryption.
    This system addresses the key distribution problem of symmetric key encryption, where the same key is
    used for both encryption and decryption.
    wiuñ;sl .=ma; fla;kh" fmdÿ h;=re .=ma;fla;kh f,io ye¢kafõ" th wdrlaIs; ikaksfõokh i|yd
    h;=re hq.,hla Ndú;d lrk ixfla;dxlk j¾.hls' fmdÿ h;=rla iy mqoa.,sl h;=rla' fuu h;=re
    .Ks;uh jYfhka iïnkaO kuq;a fjkia h' fmdÿ h;=r fla;kh i|yd Ndú;d lrk w;r mqoa.,sl
    h;=r úfla;kh i|yd Ndú;d fõ' fuu moaO;sh fla;kh iy úfla;kh hk folgu tlu h;=r
    Ndú;d lrk iuñ;sl h;=re ixfla;dxlkfha m%Odk fnodyeÍfï .eg¨j úiohs'

    Public Key - fmdÿ h;=r
    Can be freely distributed and shared with anyone.
    Used to encrypt messages or verify digital signatures.
    ´kEu flfkl=g ksoyfia fnod yeßh yel'
    mKsúv fla;kh lsÍug fyda wxls; w;aika i;Hdmkh lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs'

    Private Key - mqoa.,sl h;=r
    Kept secret and secure by the owner.
    Used to decrypt messages or create digital signatures.
    whs;slre úiska ryis.;j iy iqrlaIs;j ;nd we;'
    mKsúv úfla;kh lsÍug fyda wxls; w;aika iE§ug Ndú;d lrhs'

    How It Works - th l%shd lrk wdldrh

    Encryption - fla;kh
    •   The sender obtains the recipient’s public key.
        hjkakd ,nkakdf.a fmdÿ h;=r ,nd .kS'
    •   The sender encrypts the message using the recipient’s public key.
        hjkakd ,nkakdf.a fmdÿ h;=r Ndú;fhka mKsúvh fla;kh lrhs'
    •   The encrypted message, called ciphertext, is sent to the recipient.
        fla;dxlkh f,i ye¢kafjk fla;d;aul mKsúvh ,nkakd fj; hjkq ,efí'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                              -166-                                          #ictfromabc


    Decryption - úfla;kh
    •   The recipient uses their private key to decrypt the received ciphertext.
        ,enqKq fla;dxlh úfla;kh lsÍug ,nkakd ;u mqoa.,sl h;=r Ndú;d lrhs'
    •   The message is restored to its original plaintext form.
        mKsúvh tys uq,a ir, mdG wdlD;shg m%;sidOkh flf¾'

    Encryption - .=ma;fla;kh
    Encryption is the process of converting plaintext (readable data) into ciphertext (an unreadable format) to
    protect it from unauthorized access. This process ensures that only authorized parties can read the data by
    transforming it back into its original form through decryption.
    .=ma;fla;kh hkq wkjir m%fõYhlska wdrlaId lsÍu i|yd ir, mdG ^lshúh yels o;a;& fla;dxl
    mdG ^lshúh fkdyels wdlD;shla& njg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs' fuu l%shdj,sh úfla;kh yryd
    o;a; kej; tys uq,a iajrEmhg mßj¾;kh lsÍfuka n,h,;a md¾Yajhkag muKla lshúh yels nj
    iy;sl lrhs'

    There are 2 ways.
    wdldr 2ls'

    1) Symmetric Key Encryption - iuñ;sl h;=re .=ma;fla;kh
    •   Uses the same key for both encryption and decryption.
        fla;kh iy úfla;kh hk folgu tlu h;=r Ndú;d lrhs'
    •   Faster and more efficient for encrypting large amounts of data.
        úYd, o;a; m%udKhla .=ma;fla;kh lsÍu i|yd fõ.j;a yd jvd ld¾hlaIu fõ'
    •   Key distribution and management can be challenging because the same key must be securely shared
        between the sender and receiver.
        h;=r fnodyeÍu iy l<ukdlrKh wNsfhda.d;aul úh yelafla tlu h;=r hjkakd iy ,nkakd
        w;r wdrlaIs;j fnod.; hq;= neúks'

    2) Asymmetric Key Encryption - wiuñ;sl h;=re .=ma;fla;kh
    •   Uses a pair of keys a public key for encryption and a private key for decryption.
        h;=re hq.,hla Ndú;d lrhs' fla;kh i|yd fmdÿ h;=rla iy úfla;kh i|yd mqoa.,sl h;=rla'
    •   Solves the key distribution problem, as the public key can be openly shared.
        fmdÿ h;=r újD;j fnod .; yels neúka" h;=re fnod yeÍfï .eg¨j úi|hs'
    •   Slower than symmetric key encryption and typically used for smaller amounts of data or for securely
        exchanging symmetric keys.
        iuñ;sl h;=re .=ma;fla;khg jvd ukao.dó jk w;r idudkHfhka l=vd o;a; m%udKhla i|yd
        fyda iuñ;sl h;=re wdrlaIs;j yqjudre lr .ekSu i|yd Ndú;d fõ'

    If Suresh wants to send an encrypted message to Amara, he should encrypt the message using Amara’s

    fjk;a m%ldY jerÈ wehs"

 2. Which of the following is correct regarding parity bits?
    iu;a (parity) ìgq iïnkaOfhka my; ljrla ksjerÈ o@
    1) The parity bit in a set of bits is adjusted after that set of bits is communicated.
       ìgq iuqyhl iu;aj ìgqj" tu ìgq iuqyh ikaksfõokfhka miqj yß.eiaifjhs'
    2) The parity bit for a set of bits is selected to ensure the total number of 1-bits in the set is either even
       or odd. / ìgq iuqyhl iu;aj ìgqj f;dard.efkkqfha tu iuqyfha uq¿ 1-ìgq .Kk brÜfÜ fyda
       T;af;a jk mßÈh'
    3) The transmission speed of a communication is increased by parity bits.
       ikaksfõokhl iïfm%aIK fõ.h iu;aj ìgq u.ska jeä flf¾'
    4) Parity bits are added after a communication to correct errors.
       jerÈ ksjerÈ lsÍug" ikaksfõokhlska miqj iu;aj ìgq tl;= flf¾'
    5) Encryption needed in a data transmission is provided by parity bits.
       o;a; iïfm%aIKhlg wjYH .=ma; fla;kh (encryption) iu;aj ìgq u.ska iemfha'

                                                                                                      ( 2024 - 19 )
    Answer : 2 )
    Parity Bits/iu;aj ìgq(
    A parity bit is an additional bit added to a data set to detect errors during transmission by ensuring the total
    number of 1-bits in the data follows a specific parity (either even or odd).
    iu;aj ìgq hkq o;a;j, we;s uq¿ 1ys ìgq .Kk ksYaÑ; iudk;djla ^brÜfÜ fyda T;af;a& wkq.ukh
    lrk nj iy;sl lr .ekSfuka iïfm%aIKh w;r;=r fodaI y÷kd .ekSug o;a; lÜg,hlg tl;=
    lrk ,o w;sfral ìgqjls'
    Error Detection / fodaI y÷kd.ekSu(
    Parity bits are primarily used for detecting errors, not correcting them. If an error is detected, the system
    typically requests retransmission.
    iu;aj ìgq uQ,sl jYfhka Ndú;d lrkqfha fodaI y÷kd .ekSug fõ' fodaY ksjerÈ lsÍug Ndú; fkdfõ'
    fodaIhla wkdjrKh jqjfyd;a" moaO;sh idudkHfhka kej; iïfm%aIKhla b,a,d isà'

    Parity Types / iu;d j¾.:

    •   Even Parity: Ensures the total number of 1-bits in the data (including the parity bit) is even.
        brÜfÜ iu;dj( o;a;j, we;s uq¿ 1ys ìgq .Kk ^iu;aj ìgq we;=¿j& brÜfÜ nj iy;sl lrhs'
    •   Odd Parity: Ensures the total number of 1-bits in the data (including the parity bit) is odd.
        T;af;a iu;dj( o;a;j, we;s uq¿ 1ys ìgq .Kk ^iu;aj ìgq we;=¿j& T;af;a nj iy;sl lrhs'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                             -170-                                        #ictfromabc


    Practical Limitations / m%dfhda.sl iSudjka(
    Parity bits cannot detect errors if two or more bits are flipped in the data. They are also unrelated to
    encryption and can add minor overhead, reducing transmission speed.
    o;a;j, ìgq folla fyda jeä .Kkla udre jqjfyd;a iu;aj ìgqj,g fodaI y÷kd.; fkdyel' tAjd
    fla;khg o iïnkaO ke;s w;r iïfm%aIK fõ.h wvq lrñka iq¿ w;sßla; ld¾hhla tl;= l< yel'

    Therefore, the correct answer is option 2).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r úl,am 2) fõ'

 3. What is done by a modem when it receives an analog signal from a PSTN (Public Switched Telephone
    Network) line?
    fmdÿ iaúp ÿrl:k cd, u.lska m%;siu (analog) ix{djla ,o úg fudvuhlska l=ula isÿ fõ o@
    1) It amplifies the signal for better clarity.

    2) It compresses the signal for storage.
       wdphkh (storage) ioyd th ix{dj iïmSvkh (compress) lrhs'
    3) It demodulates the signal back into digital form.
       th ixLHdj h,s wxls; (digital) iajrEmhg úuQ¾ckh (demodulate) lrhs'
    4) It encrypts the signal for security.

    5) It modulates the signal further for transmission.
       iïfm%aIKh ioyd th ix{dj ;jÿrg;a uQ¾ckh (modulate) lrhs'

    Answer : 3 )

    Public Switched Telephone Network / fmdÿ iaúp ÿrl:k cd,
    The Public Switched Telephone Network (PSTN) is the traditional network for voice communication. It
    uses analog signals over landlines and circuit-switched technology to establish calls between users. It
    serves as the backbone for global voice communication.
    fmdÿ iaúp ÿrl:k cd, (PSTN) hkq yv ikaksfõokh i|yd jQ iïm%odhsl cd,hhs' th mßYS,lhka
    w;r weu;=ï ia:dms; lsÍu i|yd ia:djr ÿrl:k iy circuit-switched ;dlaIKh yryd m%;siu ix{d
    Ndú;d lrhs' fuh f.da,Sh yv ikaksfõokh i|yd fld÷ kdráh f,i fiajh lrhs'

    Modem / uQ¾clh
    A modem is a device that converts signals between analog and digital formats to enable communication
    over different mediums (ex: telephone lines).
    fudvuhla hkq úúO udOH yryd ^Wod( ÿrl:k ud¾.& ikaksfõokh il%Sh lsÍu i|yd m%;siu iy
    wxls; wdlD;s w;r ix{d mßj¾;kh lrk WmlrKhls'

    •   Modulation / uQ¾ckh
        Converts digital data into analog signals for transmission over analog mediums like PSTN.
        PSTN jeks m%;siu udOH yryd iïfm%aIKhg wxls; o;a; m%;siu ix{d njg mßj¾;kh lrhs'

    •   Demodulation / úuQ¾ckh
        Converts received analog signals back into digital data for computer processing.
        mß.Kl ieliqu i|yd" ,enqKq m%;siu ix{d kej; wxls; o;a; njg mßj¾;kh lrhs'

    Analog and Digital Signals / m%;siu iy wxls; ix{d(

    •   Analog Signals: Continuous signals used in traditional communication systems like PSTN.
        m%;siu ix{d( PSTN jeks iïm%odhsl ikaksfõok moaO;sj, Ndú;d jk ika;;sl ix{d'

    •   Digital Signals: Discrete signals used by computers and modern communication systems.
        wxls; ix{d( mß.Kl iy kùk ikaksfõok moaO;s Ndú;d lrk úúla; ix{d'

    Role of Demodulation / úuQ¾ckfha N+ñldj(
    When a modem receives an analog signal (from PSTN), it demodulates the signal, converting it into a
    digital format that can be processed by digital devices.
    fudvuhlg m%;siu ix{djla ^PSTN fj;ska& ,enqKq úg" tu ix{dj wxls; Wmdx. u.ska ielish yels
    wxls; wdlD;shla njg mßj¾;kh lrñka ix{dj úuQ¾ckh lrhs'

    Other terms / fjk;a kshuhka

        Increases signal strength but is unrelated to the conversion process.
        ix{d Yla;sh jeä lrk kuq;a mßj¾;k l%shdj,shg iïnkaO fkdfõ'

    •   Encryption / ixfla;kh(
        Secures the signal but is unrelated to signal conversion.
        ix{dj iqrlaIs; lrk kuq;a ix{d mßj¾;khg iïnkaO fkdfõ'

    •   Modulation / uQ¾ckh(
        Prepares a signal for transmission, which is done before sending, not after receiving.
        iïfm%aIKhg ix{djla iQodkï lrhs" th isÿ lrkq ,nkafka heùug fmr ñi ,eîfuka miqj fkdfõ'

    The modem demodulates the analog signal back into digital form for computer processing. So, the correct
    answer is 3).

 4. Which of the following best describes a switch in a network?
    cd,hl we;s iaúphla jvd;a fyd¢ka úia;r lrkafka my; l=uk m%ldYfhka o@
    1) It amplifies data signals for clearer transmission.
       jvd;a meyeÈ,s iïfm%aIKh i|yd th o;a; ix{d úia;drKh (amplifies) lrhs'
    2) It always broadcasts all incoming data to every device in the network.
       th iEuúgu msg;ska tk ish¨ o;a; cd,fha we;s iEu Wmdka.hlgu úldYh (broadcasts) lrhs'
    3) It compresses data for more efficient transmission.
       jvd;a ld¾hlaIu iïfm%aIKh i|yd th o;a; iïmSvkh (compress) lrhs'
    4) It directs data only to the specific device for which the data is intended.
       o;a; wfmalaYs; Wmdka.hg muKla th o;a; fhduq lrhs'
    5) It stores data for future processing.
       wkd.; ieliqï (processing) i|yd th o;a; wdph (store) lrhs'

                                                                                                 ( 2024 - 21 )

    Answer : 4 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    A switch is a networking device that operates primarily at the data link layer (Layer 2) of the OSI model.
    Switches are essential for efficient and secure communication in networks.
    iaúphla hkq OSI wdlD;sfha o;a; iïnkaOl ia:rfha (Layer 2) uQ,sl jYfhka l%shd;aul jk cd,lrK
    Wmdx.hls' cd, ;=< ld¾hlaIu iy wdrlaIs; ikaksfõokh i|yd iaúphka w;HjYH fõ'
RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -172-                                          #ictfromabc




    Its main functions include / tys m%odk ld¾hhka j,g we;=,;a jkafka(

    •   Directing data / o;a; fufyhùu
        A switch reads the MAC address of incoming data packets and forwards the data only to the specific
        device whose MAC address matches the destination address. This ensures efficient communication in
        the network.
        iaúphla uÕska meñfKk o;a; melÜ j, MAC ,smskh lshjk w;r MAC ,smskh .ukdka;
        ,smskhg .e<fmk ksYaÑ; Wmdx.hg muKla o;a; fhduq lrhs' fuh cd,h ;=< ld¾hlaIu
        ikaksfõokh iy;sl lrhs'

    •   Not broadcasting / úldYkh fkdfõ
        Unlike a hub, a switch does not send data to all devices in the network. It is more selective, reducing
        unnecessary traffic.
        kdôhla fuka fkdj" iaúphla cd,fha ish¨u Wmdx. fj; o;a; hjkafka ke;' th jvd;a f;dard
        fírd" wkjYH .ukd.ukh wvq lrhs'

    Why other options are incorrect / fjk;a úl,am jerÈ wehs(

    Option / úl,am 1)
    This is the function of a repeater or signal booster, not a switch.
    fuh mqk¾fhdaclhl fyda ix{d j¾Olhl l%shdldß;ajh ñi iaúphla fkdfõ'

    Option / úl,am 2)
    This is the behavior of a hub, not a switch. A hub sends data to all devices, whereas a switch sends it only
    to the intended recipient.
    fuh flakaøhl yeisÍu ñi iaúphla fkdfõ' kdôhla ish¨u Wmdx. fj; o;a; hjk w;r iaúphla
    uÕska th hjkafka wfmalaIs; ,nkakdg muKS'

    Option / úl,am 3)
    A switch does not compress data. Data compression is typically handled by software or specific network
    devices designed for compression.
    iaúphla o;a; iïmSvkh fkdlrhs' o;a; iïmSvkh idudkHfhka yiqrejkafka iïmSvkh i|yd ks¾udKh
    lr we;s uDÿldx. fyda úfYaIs; cd, Wmdx. u.sks'

    Option / úl,am 5)
    Switches do not store data long-term. They only temporarily hold data in their memory (buffer) while it is
    being processed and forwarded.
    iaúphka È.= ld,Skj o;a; .nvd fkdlrhs' Tjqka o;a; ilia lr bÈßhg hjk w;r;=r Tjqkaf.a
    u;lfha ^n*rh& ;djld,slj muKla r|jd ;nd .kS'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 4).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 4) fõ'

 5. How many usable host addresses are available in the 192.168.100.0/27 IP address block ?
    192.168.100.0/27 IP fhduq ldKavfha Ndú;hg .; yels i;aldrl fhduq fldmuK mj;So ?

    1) 16                2) 30                 3) 32                 4) 62                 5) 64

                                                                                                   ( 2024 - 22 )
    Answer : 2 )

    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    An IP address block with a /27 subnet mask means that the first 27 bits of the IP address are reserved for
    the network portion, leaving the remaining 5 bits for the host portion.
    /27 Wmcd, wdjrKhla iys; IP ,smsk íf,dla tlla hkq IP ,smskfha m<uq ìgq 27 cd, fldgi i|yd
    fjka lr we;s w;r b;sß ìgq 5 Odrl fldgi i|yd b;sß fõ'

 ➢ Step / mshjr 1:
    Calculate the total number of addresses
    iïmQ¾K ,smsk .Kk .Kkh lrkak
    The total number of IP addresses in the subnet can be calculated as 25 = 32
    Wmcd,fha we;s uq¿ IP ,smsk .Kk 25 = 32 f,i .Kkh l< yel

 ➢ Step / mshjr 2:
    Reserve addresses for network and broadcast
    cd,h iy úldYkh i|yd ,smsk fjkalr .kak
    In any subnet, two IP addresses are reserved,
    ´kEu Wmcd,hl" IP ,smsk folla fjka lr we;"
    The network address, which represents the subnet itself (in this case, 192.168.100.0). The broadcast
    address, which is used to communicate with all devices in the subnet (in this case, 192.168.100.31). These
    two addresses cannot be assigned to hosts.
    Wmcd,hu ksfhdackh lrk cd, ,smskh ^fuu wjia:dfõ§" 192.168.100.0& Wmcd,fha ish¨u Wmdx.
    iuÕ ikaksfõokh lsÍug Ndú;d lrk úldYk ,smskh ^fuu wjia:dfõ§" 192.168.100.31& fuu ,smsk
    fol Odrlhkag mejßh fkdyel'

 ➢ Step / mshjr 3:
    Calculate usable host addresses
    Ndú;d l< yels Odrl ,smsk .Kkh lrkak
    The number of usable host addresses is the total number of addresses minus the reserved addresses which
    is 32−2 = 30. These 30 usable IP addresses can be assigned to devices such as computers, printers, or other
    networked devices within the subnet.
    Ndú; l< yels Odrl ,smsk ixLHdj 32−2 = 30 jk fjkalr we;s ,smskj,ska wvq lrk ,o iïmQ¾K
    ,smsk .Kkhs' fuu Ndú; l< yels IP ,smsk 30 mß.Kl" uqøK hka;%" fyda Wmcd,h ;=< we;s fjk;a
    cd,.; Wmdx. jeks Wmdx.j,g mejßh yel'

    Therefore, the correct answer is 2).
    tneúka" ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 2) fõ'

 6. Which of the following are properties of the Transmission Control Protocol (TCP)?
    iïfm%aIK md,k kshudj,sfha (TCP) .=Kdx. my; tAjdfhka ljrlao @
    A - Detection and correction of any errors in a communication
        ikaksfõokhl fodaI we;akï tAjd y÷kd.ekSu yd ksjerÈ lsÍu
    B - Receiver acknowledging to the sender about the receipt of a data packet
        o;a; melÜgqjla ,enqKq nj ,nkakd (receiver) tjkakdg (sender) oekaùu
    C - Ensuring data packets are received in order
        o;a; melÜgq ms<sfj<ska ,efnk njg iy;sl ùu

    1) A only.          2) A and B only.     3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.    5) All of the above.
       A muKs.             A yd B muKs.         A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.        by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                                  ( 2024 - 23 )
    Answer : 4 / 5 )

    Explanation of each statement / tla tla m%ldYh meyeÈ,s lsÍu(

    Statement / m%ldYh A
    Error Detection: TCP uses checksums for error detection. Each TCP segment includes a checksum field,
    which helps identify corrupted data during transmission. If a checksum mismatch occurs, TCP discards
    the segment and requests retransmission.
    fodaI y÷kd.ekSu( TCP fodaI y÷kd.ekSu i|yd fplaiï Ndú;d lrhs' iEu TCP fldgilgu checksum
    lafIa;%hla we;=<;a jk w;r th iïfm%aIKh w;r;=r ¥Is; o;a; y÷kd .ekSug WmldÍ fõ' fplaiï
    fkd.e,mSula isÿjqjfyd;a" TCP tu fldgi bj; oud kej; iïfm%aIKh b,a,d isà'

    Error Correction: While TCP does not correct errors in the traditional sense, it corrects communication
    issues through its retransmission mechanism. If an error is detected (ex: a packet is missing or
    corrupted), TCP ensures that the packet is resent until it is received correctly. So according to this
    argument A is correct.
    fodaI ksjerÈ lsÍu( TCP iïm%odhsl w¾:fhka fodaI ksjerÈ fkdlrk w;r" th tys kej; iïfm%aIK
    hdka;%Kh yryd ikaksfõok .eg¿ ksjerÈ lrhs' fodaIhla wkdjrKh jqjfyd;a ^Wod( melÜgqjla
    w;=reoyka ù fyda ¥Is; ù we;&" TCP th ksjerÈj ,efnk f;la melÜgqj kej; hjk nj iy;sl
    lrhs' tneúka fuu ;¾lhg wkqj A ksjerÈhs'
    Or
    While TCP does have error detection mechanisms, it doesn't have the capability to correct errors directly.
    If an error is detected, TCP retransmits the lost or corrupted packet. According to this argument A is
    incorrect.
    TCP i;=j fodaI y÷kd.ekSfï hdka;%Khka we;s w;r" thg iDcqju fodaI ksjerÈ lsÍfï yelshdjla
    fkdue;' fodaIhla wkdjrKh jqjfyd;a" ke;sjQ fyda ¥Is; melÜgqj TCP kej; iïfm%aIKh lrhs' fuu
    ;¾lhg wkqj A jerÈhs'

    Statement / m%ldYh B
    This is a fundamental property of TCP. It uses a mechanism called acknowledgements (ACKs) to confirm
    the successful receipt of data packets. This ensures reliable delivery.Therefore this statement is Correct.
    fuh TCP ys uQ,sl foam,ls' o;a; melÜ id¾:l f,i ,eîu ;yjqre lsÍu i|yd th acknowledgements
    (ACKs) kï hdka;%Khla Ndú;d lrhs' fuh úYajdiodhl fnod yeÍu iy;sl lrhs' tneúka fuu m%ldYh
    ksjer§hs'

    Statement / m%ldYh C
    TCP guarantees the ordered delivery of data packets. It numbers each packet and reassembles them in the
    correct order at the receiver's end. Therefore this statement is Correct.
    weKjqï l< o;a; melÜ fnod yeÍu TCP iy;sl lrhs' th iEu melÜgqjlau wxlkh lr tAjd
    .%dylhdf.a fl<jfra ksjerÈ wkqms<sfj,g kej; tl,ia lrhs' tneúka fuu m%ldYh ksjer§hs'

    Therefore, the correct answer can be 4) or 5) based on the above two arguments.
    tneúka" by; ;¾l fol u; mokïj ksjerÈ ms<s;=r 4) fyda 5) fõ'
